367. The position of Papal Legate does not cease when the Apostolic See becomes vacant, unless otherwise determined in the pontifical letters; It ceases at the end of the term of office, by revocation communicated to the interested party and by resignation accepted by the Roman Pontiff.

368
368. Particular churches, in which and from which the one and only Catholic Church exists, are mainly the dioceses to which, if nothing else is established, the territorial prelature and the territorial abbey, the apostolic vicariate and the apostolic prefecture as well as the apostolic administration established in a stable manner are assimilated.

369
369. The diocese is a portion of the people of God, whose pastoral care is entrusted to the Bishop with the cooperation of the presbytery, so that, united to its pastor and gathered by him in the Holy Spirit through the Gospel and the Eucharist, it constitutes a particular Church, in which the Church of Christ is truly present and acts, a holy, catholic and apostolic one.

370
370. The territorial prelature or the territorial abbey is a certain portion of the people of God, territorially delimited, whose care is entrusted, by special circumstances, to a Prelate or an Abbot, who governs it as its own shepherd, in the same way as a diocesan Bishop.

372
372. § 1 . As a general rule, the portion of the people of God that constitutes a diocese or other particular Church must be circumscribed within a certain territory, so that it includes all the faithful who live there.
§ 2 . However, when it is useful in the opinion of the supreme authority of the Church, after hearing the interested Episcopal Conferences, separate particular Churches may be erected within the same territory due to the rite of the faithful or for another similar reason.

373
373. It corresponds only to the supreme authority to erect particular Churches, which once they have been legitimately erected, enjoy legal personality by virtue of the right itself.

374
374. § 1 . Every diocese or any other particular Church must be divided into distinct parts or parishes.
§ 2 . To facilitate pastoral care through a common activity, several parishes close to each other can join together in peculiar groups, such as archpriests.

375
375. § 1 . The Bishops, who by divine institution are the successors of the Apostles, by virtue of the Holy Spirit given to them, are constituted as Pastors in the Church so that they too are teachers of doctrine, priests of sacred worship and ministers for the government.
§ 2 . Through episcopal consecration, along with the function of sanctifying, Bishops also receive the functions of teaching and ruling, which, however, by their very nature, can only be exercised in hierarchical communion with the head and members of the College.

376
376. The Bishops who have been entrusted with the care of a diocese are called diocesans; the rest are called holders.

377
377. § 1 . The Supreme Pontiff freely appoints Bishops, or confirms those who have been legitimately elected.
§ 2 . At least every three years, the Bishops of the ecclesiastical province or, where circumstances so advise, those of the Episcopal Conference, must prepare by common agreement and under secrecy a list of priests, also from among the members of institutes of consecrated life, who are most suitable for the episcopate, and must send this list to the Apostolic See, remaining firm the right of each Bishop to make known particularly to the See Apostolic names of priests whom he considers worthy and suitable for the episcopal office.
§ 3 . Unless it is legitimately established otherwise, when a diocesan Bishop or a coadjutor Bishop is to be appointed, to propose a shortlist to the Apostolic See, it is the responsibility of the Papal Legate to investigate separately and communicate to the same Apostolic See, together with his opinion, what the Archbishop and the Suffragans of the province, to which the diocese that is to be provided for or with which it is located, belongs. grouped, as well as the president of the Episcopal Conference; Let the Papal Legate also hear some from the college of consultants and the cathedral chapter and, if he deems it appropriate, secretly and separately ask for the opinion of some of both clergy, and also of lay people who stand out for their wisdom.
§ 4 . If it has not been legitimately provided otherwise, the diocesan Bishop who considers that an auxiliary should be given to his diocese will propose to the Apostolic See a list of at least three of the priests who are most suitable for that office.
§ 5 . Henceforth no right or privilege of election, appointment, presentation and designation of Bishops will be granted to the civil authorities.

378
378. § 1 . For the suitability of candidates to the Episcopate, the interested party must be:
1 distinguished for the firmness of his faith, good morals, piety, zeal for souls, wisdom, prudence and human virtues, and endowed with the other qualities that make him suitable to exercise the office in question;
2 of good reputation;
3 at least thirty-five years old;
4 ordained priest for at least five years;
5 doctor or at least a graduate in sacred Scripture, theology or canon law, from an institute of higher studies approved by the Apostolic See, or at least truly expert in these disciplines.
§ 2 . The final judgment on the suitability of the candidate corresponds to the Apostolic See.

379
379. Unless he is legitimately prevented, he who has been promoted to the Episcopate must receive episcopal consecration within a period of three months from the day on which the apostolic letters reached him; and, in any case, before taking up his position.

380
380. Before taking canonical possession of his office, he who has been promoted must make the profession of faith and take the oath of fidelity to the Apostolic See, according to the formula approved by the Apostolic See itself.

381
381. § 2, excluding the general and episcopal Vicar, unless they have a special mandate.
381. § 1 . In the diocese in which he has been entrusted, the diocesan Bishop has all the ordinary, proper and immediate power required for the exercise of his pastoral function, with the exception of those causes that by law or by decree of the Supreme Pontiff are reserved to the supreme authority or to another ecclesiastical authority.
§ 2 . Unless otherwise stated by the nature of the matter or by legal prescription, those who preside over other communities of faithful mentioned in c. 368.
381. § 2, they acquire the property in favor of the particular Church; The others acquire it for the institute or for the Holy See, depending on whether the institute is capable of possessing it or not;
2 if he did not lose control of the goods through profession, he recovers the use, usufruct and administration of the goods he had, and fully acquires for himself those others that he obtains subsequently;
3 in both cases, you must dispose according to the will of the donors of those assets that you do not acquire in consideration of your person.

382
382. § 1 . He who has been promoted to the Episcopate should not interfere in the exercise of the office entrusted to him, before taking canonical possession of the diocese; He can, however, exercise the offices that he already had in the same diocese when he was promoted, without prejudice to the provisions of the c

383
383. § 1 . In exercising his pastoral function, the diocesan Bishop must be solicitous with all the faithful entrusted to him, whatever their age, condition or nationality, whether they live in the territory or if they are there temporarily, expressing his apostolic zeal also to those who, due to their circumstances, cannot sufficiently obtain the fruits of ordinary pastoral care, as well as to those who have distanced themselves from the practice of religion.
§ 2 . If there are faithful of another rite in your diocese, provide for their spiritual needs through priests or parishes of this rite, or through an Episcopal Vicar.
§ 3 . He must be humane and charitable towards brothers who are not in full communion with the Catholic Church, also promoting ecumenism as the Church understands it.
§ 4 . Consider that the unbaptized are entrusted to him in the Lord, so that the charity of Christ may also shine before them, of whom the Bishop must be a witness before men.

384
384. The diocesan Bishop attends with particular concern to the priests, whom he must listen to as his collaborators and advisors, defend their rights and ensure that they duly fulfill the obligations of their state, and that they have at their disposal those means and institutions that they need for the increase of their spiritual and intellectual life; and also ensure that their honest support and social assistance is provided, in accordance with the rule of law.

385
385. The diocesan Bishop should encourage with all his might vocations to the various ministries and consecrated life, devoting special attention to priestly and missionary vocations.

386
386. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop must teach and explain to the faithful the truths of faith that must be believed and lived, preaching frequently personally; Also take care that the prescriptions of the canons on the ministry of the word, mainly on the homily and the teaching of the catechism, are diligently fulfilled, so that the entirety of Christian doctrine is taught to all.
§ 2 . Defend with strength, in the most appropriate way, the integrity and unity of the faith, nevertheless recognizing the just freedom to investigate the truth more deeply.

387
387. The diocesan Bishop, aware that he is obliged to set an example of holiness with his charity, humility and simplicity of life, must try with all his strength to promote the holiness of the faithful, according to each one's own vocation; and, as the principal dispenser of the mysteries of God, he must incessantly ensure that the faithful entrusted to him grow in grace through the celebration of the sacraments, and know and live the paschal mystery.

388
388. § 1 . Once he has taken possession of the diocese, the diocesan Bishop must apply for the people entrusted to him the Mass every Sunday and other holy days of obligation in his region.
§ 2 . On the days indicated in the § 1, the Bishop must personally celebrate and apply the Mass for the people; and if he cannot celebrate it due to legitimate impediment, he will apply it on those same days through another person, or on other days personally.
§ 3 . The Bishop to whom, in addition to his own, other dioceses are entrusted, even by way of administration, fulfills this duty by applying a single Mass for the entire people entrusted to him.
§ 4 . The Bishop who has stopped fulfilling the obligation referred to in §§ 1 -3 must, as soon as possible, apply for the people as many Masses as he has stopped offering.

389
389. He frequently presides over the celebration of the Holy Eucharist in the cathedral or in another Church of his diocese, especially on holy days of obligation and other solemnities.

390
390. The diocesan Bishop can celebrate pontificals throughout his diocese; but not outside his own diocese without the express or at least reasonably presumed consent of the local Ordinary.

391
391. § 1 . It is the responsibility of the diocesan Bishop to govern the particular Church entrusted to him with legislative, executive and judicial power, in accordance with the law.
§ 2 . The Bishop personally exercises legislative power; The executive is exercised by himself or through the general or episcopal Vicars, in accordance with the rule of law; the judicial both personally and through the judicial Vicar and the judges, in accordance with the rule of law.

392
392. § 1 . Since he has the obligation to defend the unity of the universal Church, the Bishop must promote the discipline that is common to the entire Church, and therefore demand compliance with all ecclesiastical laws.
§ 2 . He must ensure that abuses are not introduced into ecclesiastical discipline, especially regarding the ministry of the word, the celebration of sacraments and sacramentals, the worship of God and the Saints and the administration of goods.

393
393. The diocesan Bishop represents the diocese in all its legal affairs.

394
394. § 1 . The Bishop should promote the different forms of apostolate in the diocese, and ensure that, throughout the diocese or in its particular districts, all apostolate activities are coordinated under his direction, respecting the proper character of each one.
§ 2 . Urge the faithful to fulfill their duty to carry out apostolate according to the condition and capacity of each one, and exhort them to participate in the various apostolate initiatives and provide them with help, according to the needs of place and time.

395
395. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop, even if he has a coadjutor or auxiliary, is bound by the law of personal residence in the diocese.
§ 2 . Apart from absences due to the ad limina visit, his duty to attend Councils, the Synod of Bishops and the meetings of the Episcopal Conference, or to fulfill another office that has been legitimately entrusted to him, he may be absent from his diocese with reasonable cause for no more than one month continuously or with interruptions, provided that he ensures that the diocese does not suffer any damage due to his absence.
§ 3 . He must not be absent from his diocese on Christmas, Holy Week and the Resurrection of the Lord, Pentecost and Corpus Christi, unless there is a serious and urgent cause.
§ 4 . If a Bishop is illegitimately absent from the diocese for more than six months, the Metropolitan will report this fact to the Apostolic See; If the Metropolitan is absent, the oldest of the suffragans will do the same.

396
396. § 1 . The Bishop has the obligation to visit the diocese every year totally or partially so that at least every five years he visits the entire diocese, personally or, if he is legitimately prevented, through the coadjutor Bishop, or the auxiliary, or the general or episcopal Vicar, or another priest.
§ 2 . The Bishop may choose the clerics he wishes to accompany him and help him during the visit, and any privilege or custom against this will be disapproved.

397
397. § 1 . Persons, Catholic institutions, things and sacred places that are within the scope of the diocese are subject to the ordinary episcopal visitation.
§ 2 . Only in cases determined by law can the Bishop make this visit to the members of the religious institutes of pontifical right and their homes.

398
398. Let the Bishop try to carry out the canonical visitation with due diligence: and take care not to be annoying or burdensome to anyone with unnecessary expenses.

399
399. § 1 . Every five years the diocesan Bishop must present to the Roman Pontiff a report on the situation of his diocese, according to the model determined by the Apostolic See and in the time established by it.
§ 2 . If the year established for presenting the report coincides in whole or in part with the first two years since he assumed the government of the diocese, the Bishop may for that time dispense with preparing and presenting the report.

400
400. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop, in the year in which he must present the report to the Supreme Pontiff, goes to Rome, unless the Apostolic See has been established otherwise, to venerate the tombs of the Holy Apostles Peter and Paul, and present himself to the Roman Pontiff.
§ 2 . The Bishop must personally fulfill this obligation, unless he is legitimately prevented: in this case he will do so through the coadjutor, if he has one, or the auxiliary, or a suitable priest from his presbytery, who resides in his diocese.
§ 3 . The Apostolic Vicar can fulfill this obligation through a procurator, even one who lives in Rome; The Apostolic Prefect does not have this obligation.

401
401. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop who has reached the age of seventy-five is requested to present the resignation of his office to the Supreme Pontiff, who will provide for it taking into account all the circumstances.
§ 2 . The diocesan Bishop is strongly requested to present his resignation from his office if due to illness or other serious cause his ability to perform it is diminished.
401. and 402 § 2 .

402
402. § 1 . The Bishop whose resignation from his office has been accepted retains the title of resigned Bishop of his diocese, and, if he wishes, may continue to reside there, unless in cases determined by special circumstances the Apostolic See provides otherwise.
§ 2 . The Episcopal Conference must ensure that the necessary provisions are made for the convenient and dignified support of the resigning Bishop, taking into account that the main obligation falls on the same diocese that he served.
402. § 2, unless your own institute wishes to take charge of that support; Otherwise, the Apostolic See will provide otherwise.

403
403. § 1 . When the pastoral needs of a diocese make it advisable, one or more auxiliary Bishops will be constituted, at the request of the diocesan Bishop; The auxiliary Bishop does not have the right of succession.
§ 2 . When more serious circumstances arise, also of a personal nature, the diocesan Bishop may be given an auxiliary Bishop endowed with special faculties.
§ 3 . If it seems more appropriate to the Holy See, it may appoint on its own initiative a coadjutor Bishop, also endowed with special faculties; The Coadjutor Bishop has the right of succession.
403. § 2, assist the diocesan Bishop in the entire government of the diocese, and take his place when he is absent or impeded.
403. § 2, must be appointed Vicar General by the diocesan Bishop; Furthermore, the diocesan Bishop must entrust to him, before others, everything that by law requires a special mandate.
§ 2 . Unless otherwise established in the apostolic letters and without prejudice to what is prescribed by § 1, the diocesan Bishop must appoint the auxiliary, or auxiliaries, Vicars General or, at least, Episcopal Vicars, who depend exclusively on his authority or that of the Coadjutor Bishop or Auxiliary Bishop referred to in c.
403. § 2 .
403. § 2 , they must consult each other in matters of greatest importance.
§ 2 . It is advisable that the diocesan Bishop, when resolving the most important matters, especially of a pastoral nature, consult the auxiliary Bishops before others.
§ 3 . The Coadjutor Bishop and the Auxiliary Bishop, because they are called to participate in the request of the diocesan Bishop, must exercise their functions in union of action and intentions with him.

404
404. § 1 . The Coadjutor Bishop takes possession of his office when he personally, or through a procurator, presents the apostolic letters of his appointment to the diocesan Bishop and the college of consultants, in the presence of the chancellor of the curia, who draws up a record.
§ 2 . The auxiliary bishop takes possession of his office when he presents the apostolic letters of his appointment to the diocesan bishop, in the presence of the chancellor of the curia, who draws up minutes.
§ 3 . In the event that the diocesan Bishop is totally prevented, it is sufficient for the coadjutor or auxiliary Bishop to present the apostolic letters of his appointment to the college of consultants in the presence of the chancellor of the curia.

405
405. § 1 . The Coadjutor Bishop, and also the Auxiliary Bishop, have the rights and obligations that are determined in the canons that follow, and those established in the letters of their appointment.
§ 2 . The Coadjutor Bishop and the Auxiliary Bishop, which is discussed in c.

406
406. § 1 . The Coadjutor Bishop, as well as the Auxiliary Bishop mentioned in the c
406. and 409, due to removal ordered by the Bishop or when the episcopal see is vacant.
§ 2 . Once the diocesan Bishop is suspended from office, the power of the Vicar General and the Episcopal Vicar is suspended, unless they are Bishops.

407
407. § 1 . To promote as much as possible the present and future good of the diocese, the diocesan Bishop, the coadjutor and the auxiliary Bishop referred to in the c

408
408. § 1 . If they are not justly prevented, the Coadjutor Bishop and the Auxiliary Bishop have the duty to celebrate pontificals and other functions that constitute an obligation of the diocesan Bishop, as many times as the latter requests.
§ 2 . The diocesan Bishop should not habitually entrust to another those episcopal rights and functions that the coadjutor or auxiliary Bishop can exercise.

409
409. § 2 .
§ 2 . Unless he is legitimately prevented, he who has been promoted to the office of diocesan Bishop must take canonical possession of his diocese within a period of four months from the moment in which he receives the apostolic letters, if he had not yet received episcopal consecration, and within a period of two months, if he was already consecrated.
§ 3 . The Bishop takes canonical possession of his diocese as soon as in the same diocese, personally or through a procurator, he shows the apostolic letters to the college of consultants, in the presence of the chancellor of the curia, who draws up a record, or, in newly erected dioceses, when he makes the clergy and people present in the cathedral church aware of those letters, with the oldest priest among those attending drawing up a record.
§4 . It is highly advisable that the canonical inauguration takes place in the cathedral church, with a liturgical act attended by the clergy and the people.
409. § 1 . When the episcopal see becomes vacant, the coadjutor Bishop immediately becomes Bishop of the diocese for which he was appointed, provided he has already legitimately taken possession.
§ 2 . If the competent authority has not established otherwise, when the episcopal see becomes vacant and until the new Bishop takes possession of the diocese, the auxiliary Bishop retains all and only those powers and faculties that as Vicar General or Episcopal Vicar he had when the see was covered; and if he has not been chosen for the function of diocesan Administrator, he exercises that power of his, which the right confers on him, under the authority of the diocesan Administrator who is in charge of the diocese.
409. § 2 ;
2 receives full remuneration for his job.

410
410. The coadjutor Bishop and the auxiliary Bishop, as well as the diocesan Bishop, have the duty to reside in the diocese, from which they should not be absent except for a short time, except when they have to fulfill an office outside the diocese or on vacation, which should not last more than one month.

411
411. As regards the resignation of the office, the prescriptions of the ccs apply to the coadjutor and auxiliary Bishop.

412
412. The episcopal see is considered impeded when, due to captivity, relegation, banishment or incapacity, the diocesan Bishop is totally unable to exercise his pastoral function in the diocese, so that not even by letter can he communicate with his diocesans.

413
413. § 1 . Unless the Holy See has provided otherwise, when a see is prevented, the government of the diocese is the responsibility of the Coadjutor Bishop if he is present; and if it does not exist or is prevented, to an auxiliary Bishop or Vicar general or episcopal, or to another priest in accordance with the order established in a list that must be drawn up by the diocesan Bishop as soon as possible, once he has taken possession of the diocese; This list, which must be communicated to the Metropolitan, will be renewed at least every three years, and will be kept secret by the chancellor.
§ 2 . If there is no coadjutor Bishop or he is prevented, and the list mentioned in the § 1 is not provided, it is up to the college of consultants to choose a priest to govern the diocese.
§ 3 . Whoever takes charge of the regime of the diocese in accordance with §§ 1 or 2, must inform the Holy See as soon as possible that the diocese is impeded and that he has assumed its government.

414
414. Anyone who, in accordance with the norm of c. 413, has been called to temporarily exercise the pastoral care of the diocese while it is impeded, has in his pastoral function the obligations and power that by right belong to a diocesan Administrator.

415
415. If, due to an ecclesiastical penalty, the diocesan Bishop is prevented from exercising his function, the Metropolitan or, failing that or in the case of himself, the most senior of the suffragans according to the order of promotion, will immediately appeal to the Holy See, so that it provides.

416
416. An episcopal see becomes vacant due to the death of the Bishop, resignation accepted by the Roman Pontiff, transfer and deprivation notified to the Bishop.

417
417. All acts carried out by the Vicar General or by the Episcopal Vicar are valid, until they have received certain news of the death of the diocesan Bishop, and the acts carried out by the Diocesan Bishop or by the Vicar General or Episcopal are equally valid, until the moment in which they receive certain news of the aforementioned pontifical acts.

418
418. § 1 . From the moment he receives certain news of his transfer, the Bishop must go to the ad quam diocese within two months, and take canonical possession of it, and the diocese to which it becomes vacant at the time he takes possession of the new one.
§ 2 . From the day he receives certain news of his transfer until he takes canonical possession of the new diocese, in the diocese to which the Bishop transferred:
1 has the power and duties of a diocesan Administrator, and all power of the Vicar General and the Episcopal Vicar ceases except as indicated in c.

419
419. When the see becomes vacant and until the constitution of the diocesan Administrator, the government of the diocese passes to the auxiliary Bishop or, if there are several, to the most senior of them in the order of their promotion, and, where there is no auxiliary Bishop, to the college of consultants, unless the Holy See has established otherwise. Whoever thus takes charge of the government of the diocese must convene without delay the college that is competent to appoint the diocesan Administrator.

420
420. When the see becomes vacant in a vicariate or apostolic prefecture, the Provicar or Proprefect, appointed exclusively for this purpose by the Vicar or Prefect, takes charge of the government immediately after the canonical takeover, unless the Holy See has determined otherwise.

421
421. § 1 . The diocesan Administrator, that is, the one who must temporarily govern the diocese, must be elected by the college of consultants within eight days from the moment in which it receives news of the vacancy of the see, without prejudice to what is prescribed by the c
421. § 2 and 425 § 3 .
§ 2 . When circumstances require it, the Metropolitan may receive from the Holy See special orders and powers that will be determined by particular law.
§ 3 . No other power of regime belongs to the Metropolitan over the suffragan dioceses; but he can perform sacred functions in all churches, just like the Bishop in his own diocese, prior warning to the diocesan Bishop, when it comes to the cathedral church.

422
422. The auxiliary Bishop and, failing that, the college of consultants, inform the Apostolic See as soon as possible of the death of the Bishop; and whoever has been elected Diocesan Administrator must do the same with respect to his appointment.

423
423. §1 . Any contrary custom being disapproved, a single diocesan Administrator must be appointed; Otherwise, the election is void.
§2 . The diocesan Administrator must not be at the same time a treasurer; Therefore, if the treasurer is appointed Administrator, the economic affairs council will provisionally elect another treasurer.

424
424. The diocesan Administrator must be chosen in accordance with the norm of ccs. 165-178.

425
425. § 1 . Only a priest who has reached the age of thirty-five and has not been elected, appointed or presented for the same vacant see can be validly appointed to the position of Diocesan Administrator.
§ 2 . A priest who stands out for his doctrine and prudence must be chosen as Diocesan Administrator.
§ 3 . If the conditions established in the § 1 have not been respected, the Metropolitan, or the oldest suffragan according to the order of promotion in the case of the Metropolitan Church, will appoint the Administrator for that time, after verifying the facts; The acts carried out by whoever has been elected against what is prescribed by § 1 are null and void by virtue of the law itself.

426
426. While the see is vacant, whoever governs the diocese, before being appointed Diocesan Administrator, has the power that the law attributes to the Vicar General.

427
427. § 1 . The diocesan Administrator has the duties and enjoys the power of the diocesan Bishop, with the exclusion of everything that by its very nature or by the law itself is excepted.
§ 2 . The diocesan Administrator acquires his power by the very fact of having accepted his election, and no confirmation is required from anyone, remaining firm the obligation prescribed by c. 833, 4.

428
428. § 1 . With the headquarters vacant, nothing should be innovated.
§ 2 . Those who temporarily take charge of the regime of the diocese are prohibited from carrying out any act that may cause harm to the diocese or episcopal rights; Specifically, they and any others, personally or through others, are prohibited from stealing, destroying or altering any document of the diocesan curia.

429
429. The diocesan Administrator is obliged to reside in the diocese and to apply the Mass for the people in accordance with the norm of c. 388.

430
430. § 1 . The diocesan Administrator ceases to hold office when the new Bishop takes possession of the diocese.
§ 2 . The removal of the diocesan Administrator is reserved to the Holy See; The resignation, if applicable, must be submitted authentically to the college competent for its election, but does not need its acceptance; In case of removal or resignation of the diocesan Administrator, or if he dies, another diocesan Administrator will be elected, in accordance with the norm of c. 421.

431
431. § 1 . To promote common pastoral action in several neighboring dioceses, according to the circumstances of the people and places, and so that reciprocal relations between the diocesan Bishops are more adequately fostered, the particular Churches will be grouped into ecclesiastical provinces delimited territorially.
§ 2 . As a general rule, there will be no exempt dioceses from now on; Therefore, all dioceses and other particular Churches that are within the territory of an ecclesiastical province must be attached to that province.
§ 3 . It corresponds exclusively to the supreme authority of the Church, after hearing the interested Bishops, to constitute, suppress or change the provinces.

432
432. § 1 . In the ecclesiastical province, the Provincial Council and the Metropolitan Council have authority, in accordance with the rule of law.
§ 2 . The province has, in its own right, legal personality.

433
433. § 1 . If it seems useful, especially in nations where there are more
Particular churches, the closest ecclesiastical provinces can be constituted by the Holy See into ecclesiastical regions, at the proposal of the Episcopal Conference.
§ 2 . The ecclesiastical region can be established as a legal entity.

434
434. It is the responsibility of the assembly of Bishops of an ecclesiastical region to promote cooperation and common pastoral action in the region; However, the powers that in the canons of this Code are attributed to the Episcopal Conference do not correspond to the aforementioned assembly, unless the Holy See grants it some in a special way.

435
435. The Metropolitan presides over the ecclesiastical province, who is in turn Archbishop of the diocese entrusted to him; This office is attached to an episcopal see determined or approved by the Roman Pontiff.

436
436. § 1 . In the suffragan dioceses, the Metropolitan is responsible for:
1 to ensure that faith and ecclesiastical discipline are diligently preserved, and to inform the Roman Pontiff about abuses if any;
2 make the canonical visitation if the suffragan had neglected it, with a cause previously approved by the Apostolic See;
3 appoint the diocesan Administrator, in accordance with cc.

437
437. § 1 . Within a period of three months from the episcopal consecration, or from the canonical provision, if he had already been consecrated, the Metropolitan, personally or through a procurator, is obliged to ask the Roman Pontiff for the pallium, which is a sign of the power with which, in communion with the Roman Church, he is invested in his own province.
§ 2 . The Metropolitan may use the pallium in accordance with the liturgical laws, in all the churches of the ecclesiastical province over which he presides, but not outside it, not even with the consent of the diocesan Bishop.
§3 . The Metropolitan needs a new canopy, if it is transferred to a different metropolitan headquarters.

438
438. Apart from the honorific prerogative, the title of Patriarch or Primate does not carry with it any power of regime in the Latin Church, unless in some case something else is established by apostolic privilege or by approved custom.

439
439. § 1 . The plenary council, for all the particular Churches of the same Episcopal Conference, must be held whenever it appears necessary or useful to that Episcopal Conference, with the approval of the Apostolic See.
§ 2 . The norm established in the § 1 also applies to the provincial council held in an ecclesiastical province whose limits coincide with those of the territory of a nation.
439. § 2 .
§ 2 . The provincial council should not be convened when the metropolitan seat is vacant.

440
440. § 1 . The provincial council for the different particular Churches of the same ecclesiastical province must be held as many times as it seems appropriate to the majority of the diocesan Bishops of the province, without prejudice to what is prescribed by the c

441
441. It corresponds to the Episcopal Conference:
1 convene the plenary council;
2 designate within the territory of the Episcopal Conference the place where the council is to be held;
3 elect from among the diocesan Bishops the president of the plenary council, which must be approved by the Apostolic See;
4 determine the regulations and the issues to be discussed, set the start date and duration of the plenary council, move it, extend it and conclude it.

442
442. § 1 . It is up to the Metropolitan, with the consent of the majority of the suffragan Bishops:
1 convene the provincial council;
2 designate the place of its celebration within the territory of the province;
3 determine the regulations and the issues to be discussed, set the start date and duration of the provincial council, move it, extend it and conclude it.
§ 2 . The presidency of the provincial council falls to the Metropolitan and, if he is legitimately prevented, to the suffragan Bishop elected by the others.

443
443. § 1 They must be summoned to particular councils and have a deliberative vote in them:
1 the diocesan Bishops;
2 the coadjutor and auxiliary Bishops;
3 other titular Bishops who perform a peculiar function in the territory, commissioned by the Apostolic See or the Episcopal Conference.
§ 2 . Other titular Bishops, even retired ones, who reside within the territory may be called to the particular councils; which have a deliberative vote.
§ 3 . The following must be summoned to particular councils with only an advisory vote:
1 the Vicars General and the Episcopal Vicars of all the particular Churches of the territory;
2 the major Superiors of religious institutes and societies of apostolic life, in numbers that will be set, for both men and women, by the Episcopal Conference or by the Bishops of the province, elected respectively by all the major Superiors of the institutes and societies based in the territory;
3 the rectors of ecclesiastical and Catholic universities and the deans of the faculties of theology and canon law, who have their headquarters in the territory;
4 some rectors of major seminaries, whose number will be determined as established in n. 2, chosen by the rectors of the seminaries in the territory.
§ 4 . Priests and some other faithful may also be called to particular councils, with an advisory vote, in such a way, however, that their number does not exceed half of those indicated in §§ 1 -3.
§ 5 . The cathedral chapters, as well as the presbyteral council and the pastoral council of each particular Church, must also be invited to the provincial councils, so that each of these institutions sends two of its members elected collegially as procurators; and these have only an advisory vote.
§ 6 . Other people may also be called to particular councils as guests, if it seems appropriate to the Episcopal Conference for the plenary council, or to the Metropolitan together with the suffragan Bishops for the provincial council.

444
444. § 1 . All those who have been summoned must attend the particular councils, unless there is a just impediment, of which they must inform the president of the council.
§ 2 . Those who have been summoned to a particular council and enjoy a deliberative vote therein may send a procurator if they are justly prevented from attending; This attorney only has an advisory vote.

445
445. The particular council ensures that the pastoral needs of the People of God are provided for in its territory, and has power of regime, especially legislative, so that, always safeguarding the universal law of the Church, it can establish whatever seems appropriate for the increase of faith, the organization of common pastoral activity, the order of good customs and the observance, establishment or protection of common ecclesiastical discipline.

446
446. Once the particular council is concluded, its president must ensure that the complete minutes of the council are sent to the Apostolic See; The decrees given by the council will not be promulgated until after they have been reviewed by the Apostolic See; It is up to the same council to determine the manner of promulgation of the decrees and the moment at which, once promulgated, they will begin to bind.

447
447. The Episcopal Conference, a permanent institution, is the assembly of the Bishops of a given nation or territory, who together exercise some pastoral functions with respect to the faithful of their territory, to promote in accordance with the rule of law the greatest good that the Church provides to men, above all through forms and modes of apostolate suitably adapted to the peculiar circumstances of time and place.

448
448. § 1 . As a general rule, the Episcopal Conference includes the prelates of all the particular Churches of the same nation, in accordance with the norm of c. 450.
§ 2 . But, if in the opinion of the Apostolic See, having heard the interested diocesan Bishops, the circumstances of people or things so advise, an Episcopal Conference can be erected for a territory of smaller or larger extension, so that it only includes the Bishops of some particular Churches existing in a certain territory, or the prelates of the particular Churches of different nations; It is up to the Apostolic See itself to give specific norms for each of these Conferences.

449
449. § 1 . It is exclusively the responsibility of the supreme authority of the Church, after hearing the interested Bishops, to erect, suppress or change the Episcopal Conferences.
§ 2 . The legitimately established Episcopal Conference has legal personality by virtue of the law itself.

450
450. § 1 . By law itself, all the diocesan Bishops of the territory and those who are equivalent to them in law belong to the Episcopal Conference, as well as the coadjutor Bishops, the auxiliary Bishops and the other titular Bishops who, by order of the Holy See or the Episcopal Conference, fulfill a peculiar function in the same territory; Ordinaries of another rite may also be invited, but only with a consultative vote, unless the statutes of the Episcopal Conference determine otherwise.
§ 2 . The other titular Bishops and the Legate of the Roman Pontiff are not de jure members of the Episcopal Conference.

451
451. Each Episcopal Conference must draw up its own statutes, which must be reviewed by the Apostolic See, in which, among other things, norms are established on the plenary assemblies of the Conference, the permanent commission of Bishops and the general secretariat of the Conference, and other offices and commissions are also established that, in the opinion of the Conference, can contribute more effectively to achieving its goal.

452
452. § 1 . Each Episcopal Conference elects its own president in accordance with the rules of the statutes, determines who is to fulfill the function of vice president when the president is legitimately prevented, and appoints the general secretary.
§ 2 . The president of the Conference or, when he is legitimately prevented, the vice president, chairs not only the general assemblies of the Conference, but also the permanent commission.

453
453. The plenary meetings of the Episcopal Conference must be held at least once a year, and also whenever special circumstances require it, according to the prescriptions of the statutes.

454
454. § 1 . In the plenary meetings of the Episcopal Conference, the diocesan Bishops and those who are equivalent to them in law, as well as the coadjutor Bishops, have a deliberative vote of their own right.
§ 2 . The auxiliary Bishops and the other titular Bishops belonging to the Episcopal Conference have a deliberative or consultative vote, as determined by the statutes of the Conference; It must be firm, however, that only those mentioned in the § 1 enjoy the deliberative vote when it comes to drafting the statutes or modifying them.

455
455. § 1 . The Episcopal Conference can issue general decrees only in cases where common law so prescribes or when so established by a special mandate of the Apostolic See, granted motu proprio or at the request of the Conference itself.
§ 2 . For the validity of the decrees discussed in the § 1, it is necessary that they be given in a plenary meeting with at least two-thirds of the votes of the Prelates who belong to the Conference with a deliberative vote, and they do not obtain binding force until, having been reviewed by the Apostolic See, they are legitimately promulgated.
§ 3 . The Episcopal Conference itself determines the mode of promulgation and the day from which the decrees come into force.
§ 4 . In cases in which neither universal law nor a peculiar mandate of the Holy See has granted the Episcopal Conference the power referred to in § 1, the competence of each diocesan Bishop remains complete and neither the Conference nor its president can act on behalf of all the Bishops unless each and every one has given their own consent.

456
456. At the conclusion of the plenary meeting of the Episcopal Conference, the president will send to the Apostolic See a list of the minutes of the Conference as well as its decrees, both so that these minutes come to the attention of the Apostolic See and so that it can review the decrees, if any.

457
457. It is the responsibility of the permanent commission of Bishops to ensure that the issues to be discussed in the plenary meeting are prepared, and that the decisions taken therein are duly executed; It is also responsible for carrying out other matters entrusted to it in accordance with the rules of the statutes.

458
458. The general secretariat is responsible for:
1 make a list of the minutes and decrees of the plenary meeting of the Conference and the acts of the permanent commission of Bishops, and transmit them to all members of the Conference; and also write other minutes assigned to him by the president of the Conference or the permanent commission;
2 communicate to the neighboring Episcopal Conferences the acts and documents whose transmission to them has been commissioned by the Conference in a plenary meeting or by the permanent commission of Bishops.

459
459. § 1 . Relations between Episcopal Conferences must be fostered, especially among those closest to each other, to promote and defend the greatest good.
§ 2 . However, the Apostolic See must be heard beforehand whenever the Episcopal Conferences do or declare something of a clearly international nature.

460
460. The diocesan synod is an assembly of priests and other faithful chosen from a particular Church, who provide their help to the Bishop of the diocese for the good of the entire diocesan community, in accordance with the canons that follow.

461
461. § 1 . In each particular Church, the diocesan synod must be held when circumstances warrant it in the judgment of the Bishop of the diocese, after hearing the presbyteral council.
§ 2 . If a Bishop is entrusted with the care of several dioceses, or is diocesan Bishop of one and Administrator of another, he can celebrate a synod for all the dioceses that have been entrusted to him.

462
462. § 1 . Only the diocesan Bishop can convene the synod, and not the one who provisionally presides over the diocese.
§ 2 . The diocesan Bishop presides over the synod, although he can delegate this function, for each of the sessions, to the Vicar General or to an Episcopal Vicar.

463
463. § 1 . They must be summoned to the diocesan synod as synodal members and have the duty to participate in it:
1 the Coadjutor Bishop and the auxiliary Bishops;
2 the Vicars General and the Episcopal Vicars, as well as the Judicial Vicar;
3 the canons of the cathedral church;
4 members of the presbyteral council;
5 lay faithful, also those who are members of institutes of consecrated life, at the election of the pastoral council, in the form and number determined by the diocesan Bishop or, in the absence of this council, in the manner determined by the Bishop;
6 the rector of the diocesan major seminary;
7 the archpriests;
8 at least one priest from each archpriest, elected by all those who have a priest of souls in him; Likewise, another priest must be chosen to eventually replace the previous one in case of impediment;
9 some Superiors of religious institutes and societies of apostolic life that have a home in the diocese, who will be elected in the number and in the manner determined by the diocesan Bishop.
§ 2 . The diocesan Bishop can also call other people to the synod as members, both clerics, members of institutes of consecrated life, and lay faithful.
§ 3 . If he deems it appropriate, the diocesan Bishop may invite to the synod, as observers, some ministers or members of Churches or ecclesial communities that are not in full communion with the Catholic Church.

464
464. If a member of the synod is legitimately prevented, he cannot send a procurator to assist on his behalf; but he must inform the diocesan Bishop about this impediment.

465
465. All proposed questions will be submitted to the free discussion of the members in the sessions of the synod.

466
466. The diocesan Bishop is the only legislator in the diocesan synod, and the other members of it have only an advisory vote; He alone signs the declarations and decrees of the synod, which can be published only by virtue of his authority.

467
467. The diocesan Bishop must transfer the text of the synodal declarations and decrees to the Metropolitan and the Episcopal Conference.

468
468. § 1 . It is up to the diocesan Bishop, according to his prudent judgment, to suspend and even dissolve the diocesan synod.
§ 2 . If the episcopal see is vacant or impeded, the diocesan synod is interrupted by its own right, until the new diocesan Bishop decrees its continuation or declares it concluded.

469
469. The diocesan curia consists of those organizations and people who collaborate with the Bishop in the government of the entire diocese, mainly in the direction of pastoral activity, in the administration of the diocese, as well as in the exercise of judicial power.

470
470. It is the responsibility of the diocesan Bishop to appoint those who are to carry out offices in the diocesan curia.

471
471. All those who are admitted to hold offices in the diocesan curia must:
1 promise that they will faithfully carry out their task, according to the manner determined by law or by the Bishop;
2 keep secret, within the limits and according to the manner established by law or by the Bishop.

472
472. Regarding the causes and people related to the exercise of judicial power in the curia, the prescriptions of Book VII of the processes must be observed; As far as the administration of the diocese is concerned, the prescriptions of the following canons will be observed.

473
473. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop must ensure that all matters relating to the administration of the entire diocese are duly coordinated, and that they are ordered in the most effective way for the good of the portion of the people of God entrusted to him.
§ 2 . It is up to the diocesan Bishop himself to coordinate the pastoral activity of the Vicars, whether general or episcopal; Where appropriate, a Moderator of the Curia may be appointed, who must be a priest, whose responsibility it is, under the authority of the Bishop, to coordinate matters pertaining to the treatment of administrative matters and also to ensure that the remaining staff of the Curia duly fulfill the office entrusted to them.
§ 3 . Unless, in the opinion of the Bishop, the circumstances of the place advise otherwise, the Vicar General or, if there are several, one of the Vicars General must be appointed Moderator of the Curia.
§ 4 . To better promote pastoral action, the Bishop may establish, if he considers it appropriate, an episcopal council, made up of the general and episcopal Vicars.

474
474. The acts of the curia intended to produce legal effect must be signed by the Ordinary from which they come, as a requirement for their validity, as well as by the chancellor of the curia or a notary; The chancellor has the obligation to inform the Moderator of the curia about these acts.

475
475. § 1 . In each diocese, the Bishop must appoint a Vicar General, who, endowed with ordinary power in accordance with the canons that follow, must assist him in the government of the entire diocese.
§ 2 . As a general rule, only one Vicar General must be appointed, unless the size of the diocese, the number of inhabitants or other pastoral reasons advise otherwise.

476
476. When the good government of the diocese requires it, the diocesan Bishop may also appoint one or more episcopal Vicars, who, either in a certain circumscription of the diocese, or for certain matters or with respect to the faithful of the same rite or for a specific group of people, have the same ordinary power that by universal right belongs to the Vicar General, in accordance with the norm of the canons that follow.

477
477. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop freely appoints the Vicar General and the Episcopal Vicar and can also freely remove them, subject to what is prescribed in c. 406; The episcopal Vicar who is not an auxiliary Bishop must be appointed only for a certain period, which will be determined in the same act of his appointment.
§ 2 . When the Vicar General is legitimately absent or impeded, the diocesan Bishop may appoint another to take his place; The same norm applies to the Episcopal Vicar.

478
478. § 1 . The Vicar General and the Episcopal Vicar must be priests, of no less than thirty years of age, doctors or graduates in canon law or theology or at least truly experts in these matters, and endowed with sound doctrine, honesty, prudence and experience in the management of affairs.
§ 2 . The position of general and episcopal vicar is incompatible with that of penitentiary canon, and cannot be entrusted to blood relatives of the Bishop up to the fourth degree.

479
479. § 1 . By virtue of his office, the Vicar General has the executive power throughout the diocese that corresponds by right to the diocesan Bishop, to carry out any type of administrative acts, except, however, those that the Bishop has reserved for himself or that, according to law, require a special mandate from the Bishop.
§ 2 . The power referred to in the § 1 belongs by right to the Episcopal Vicar, but only for that portion of territory, or with respect to those matters, or faithful of a certain rite or group, for which he has been appointed, except for any actions the Bishop has reserved for himself or the Vicar General, or which according to law require a special mandate from the Bishop.
§ 3 Within their own sphere of competence, the usual faculties granted by the Apostolic See to the Bishop, as well as the execution of the rescripts, also correspond to the Vicar General and the Episcopal Vicar, unless otherwise expressly established or the personal qualities of the diocesan Bishop have been taken into consideration.

480
480. The Vicar General and the Episcopal Vicar must inform the diocesan Bishop about the most important matters to be resolved or already resolved, and they will never act against the will and intentions of the diocesan Bishop.

481
481. § 1 . The power of the Vicar General and the Episcopal Vicar ceases upon completion of the term of his mandate, by resignation, and also, remaining safe what is prescribed by the cc

482
482. §1 . In each curia, there must be a chancellor, whose main function, unless the particular law establishes otherwise, is to ensure that the minutes of the curia are drawn up, issued and kept in the curia's archive.
§ 2 . When it seems necessary, an assistant to the chancellor, called the vice-chancellor, may be appointed.
§ 3 . The chancellor and vice-chancellor are by right notaries and secretaries of the curia.

483
483. § 1 . In addition to the chancellor, there may be other notaries, whose writing or signature gives public attestation, whether it concerns any type of act, whether only for judicial matters, or only for acts referring to a certain cause or matter.
§ 2 . The chancellor and the notaries must be people of good reputation and above all suspicion; In cases in which the good reputation of a priest may be put on trial, the notary must be a priest.

484
484. The office of notaries consists of:
1 draft the minutes and documents referring to decrees, provisions, obligations and other matters for which your intervention is required;
2 faithfully record everything done in writing, and sign it, indicating the place, day, month and year;
3 show to anyone who legitimately requests them those minutes or documents contained in the registry, and authenticate their copies by declaring them in accordance with the original.

485
485. The chancellor and other notaries can be freely removed from their office by the diocesan Bishop, but not by the diocesan Administrator without the consent of the college of consultants.

486
486. § 1 . All documents that refer to the diocese or parishes must be safeguarded with the greatest diligence.
§ 2 . A diocesan archive or tabular must be established in each curia, in a safe place, in which the documents and writings corresponding to diocesan affairs, both spiritual and temporal, are conserved in clear order and diligently kept.
§ 3 . An inventory or index must be made of the documents kept in the archive, with a brief summary of the content of each writing.

487
487. § 1 The archive must be closed, and only the Bishop and the chancellor must have the key; No one is allowed to enter it without permission from the Bishop, or the Moderator of the curia together with the chancellor.
§ 2 . All interested parties have the right to receive personally or through a solicitor, an authentic copy, written or photocopied, of those documents that, being public by their nature, refer to their personal status.

488
488. It is not permitted to remove documents from the archive, except for a short time and with the consent of the Bishop, or the Moderator of the Curia together with the chancellor.

489
489. § 1 . There must also be a secret archive in the diocesan curia, or at least a closet or a box within the general archive, completely locked and that cannot be moved from the location, where the documents that are to be kept secret are kept with great caution.
§ 2 . Every year the documents of those criminal cases in matters of customs whose inmates have already died, or that have been resolved with a conviction ten years earlier, must be destroyed, and a brief summary of the fact must be preserved along with the text of the final sentence.

490
490. § 1 . Only the Bishop has the key to the secret archive.
§ 2 . While the seat is vacant, the secret archive or closet will not be opened, except in case of true necessity, by the diocesan Administrator personally.
§ 3 . Documents should not be removed from the archive or secret closet.

491
491. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop shall ensure that the records and documents contained in the archives of the cathedral churches, collegiate churches, parishes and other churches in his territory are diligently preserved, and that inventories or indexes are made in double copies, one of which will be kept in his own archive, and the other in the diocesan archive.
§ 2 . The diocesan Bishop should also ensure that there is a historical archive in the diocese, and that documents that have historical value are carefully stored and organized in a systematic manner.
§ 3 . To examine or remove from their place the minutes and documents referred to in §§ 1 and 2, the norms established by the diocesan Bishop must be observed.

492
492. § 1 . In each diocese, a council of economic affairs must be established, chaired by the diocesan Bishop or his delegate, consisting of at least three faithful designated by the Bishop, who are truly experts in economic matters and civil law, and of proven integrity.
§ 2 . The members of the economic affairs council are appointed for a period of five years, but after that time, the appointment can be renewed for other five-year terms.
§ 3 . Relatives of the Bishop up to the fourth degree of consanguinity or affinity are excluded from the council on economic affairs.

493
493. In addition to the functions entrusted to it in Book V Of the temporal goods of the Church, it is the responsibility of the council of economic affairs, in accordance with the instructions received from the Bishop, to prepare each year the budget of income and expenses for the entire regime of the diocese in the coming year, as well as to approve the income and expense accounts at the end of the year.

494
494. § 1 . In each diocese, the Bishop, after hearing the college of consultants and the council of economic affairs, must appoint a treasurer, who is truly an expert in economic matters and of recognized honesty.
§ 2 . The treasurer must be appointed for five years, but the appointment can be renewed for other five-year periods, even more than once, upon expiration of the term; during the time of his position, he should not be removed except for serious reasons, which the Bishop must weigh after hearing the college of consultants and the council of economic affairs.
§ 3 . It is the responsibility of the treasurer, in accordance with the manner determined by the council of economic affairs, to administer the assets of the diocese under the authority of the Bishop and, with the diocese's own income, to make the expenses legitimately ordered by the Bishop or those who have been entrusted by him.
§ 4 . At the end of the year, the treasurer must report income and expenses to the economic affairs council.
494. §§ 3 and 4, the diocesan Bishop may entrust to the treasurer the functions indicated in cc. 1276 § 1 and 1279 § 2 .

495
495. § 1 . In each diocese, the presbyteral council must be constituted, that is, a group of priests that is like the Bishop's senate, representing the presbytery, whose mission is to help the Bishop in the government of the diocese in accordance with the rule of law, to provide as much as possible for the pastoral good of the portion of the people of God that has been entrusted to him.
§ 2 . In apostolic vicariates and apostolic prefectures, the Vicar or Prefect must constitute a council of at least three missionary priests, from whom he receives the opinion, even by letter, on the most serious matters.
495. § 2, the functions of the college of consultants, unless the law provides otherwise.
495. § 2, the Provicar and the Apostolic Proprefect.
§ 2 . The diocesan Administrator, the Pro-Vicar and the Apostolic Pro-Prefect must not give resignations to those who were denied access to orders by the diocesan Bishop or by the Vicar or Apostolic Prefect.

496
496. The presbyteral council must have its own statutes, approved by the diocesan Bishop, taking into account the norms given by the Episcopal Conference.

497
497. Regarding the appointment of the members of the presbyteral council:
1 Approximately half of them must be freely chosen by the priests themselves, in accordance with the norm of the canons that follow and the statutes;
2 some priests, according to the norm of the statutes, must be ex officio members, that is, they belong to the council by virtue of the office entrusted to them;
3 the Bishop has the power to freely appoint other members.

498
498. § 1 . For the constitution of the presbyteral council, the following have both active and passive right of election:
1 all secular priests incardinated in the diocese;
2 those secular priests not incardinated in the diocese, as well as priests who are members of a religious institute or a society of apostolic life, who reside in the diocese and exercise some office for the good of it.
§ 2 . When so determined by the statutes, this same right of election may be granted to other priests who have their domicile or quasi-domicile in the diocese.

499
499. The way of electing the members of the presbyteral council must be determined in the statutes, so that, to the extent possible, the priests of the presbytery are represented, taking into account above all the different ministries and the various regions of the diocese.

500
500. § 1 . It is the responsibility of the diocesan Bishop to convene the presbyteral council, preside over it and determine the issues that should be discussed or accept those proposed by the members.
§ 2 . The presbyteral council has only advisory votes; The diocesan Bishop must hear him in matters of greatest importance, but needs his consent only in cases expressly determined by law.
§ 3 . The presbyteral council can never proceed without the diocesan Bishop, who is also exclusively responsible for ensuring that what has been established in accordance with § 2 is made public.

501
501. § 1 . The members of the presbyteral council must be appointed for the term determined in the statutes, in such a way, however, that all or part of the council is renewed every five years.
§ 2 . When the seat becomes vacant, the presbyteral council ceases, and the college of consultants fulfills its functions; The Bishop must reconstitute the presbyteral council within a period of one year from the moment in which he has taken possession.
§ 3 . If the presbyteral council fails to fulfill its entrusted function for the good of the diocese or seriously abuses it, the Bishop, after consulting the Metropolitan, or, if it is the same metropolitan see, the most senior suffragan Bishop due to promotion, may dissolve it, but must constitute it again within a period of one year.

502
502. §3 .
§ 2 . If, for any reason, the diocesan Administrator is not legitimately elected within the established period, his appointment passes to the Metropolitan, and, in the event that the vacant see is precisely the metropolitan, or the metropolitan as well as a suffragan, to the most senior suffragan Bishop according to the order of promotion.
502. § 1 . Among the members of the presbyteral council, the Bishop freely appoints some priests, in number not less than six nor more than twelve, who constitute for five years the college of consultants, which is responsible for the functions determined by law; However, at the end of the five-year period it continues to exercise its functions until a new council is established.
§ 2 . The diocesan Bishop presides over the college of consultants; When the seat is impeded or vacant, the one who provisionally acts as the Bishop or, if the latter has not yet been constituted, the priest of the oldest college of consultants by his ordination.
§ 3 . The Episcopal Conference may establish that the functions of the college of consultants be entrusted to the cathedral chapter.
§ 4 . In an apostolic vicariate or apostolic prefecture, they are the responsibility of the mission council, which is discussed in c.

503
503. The chapter of canons, cathedral or collegiate, is a college of priests, which is responsible for celebrating the most solemn liturgical functions in the central church or in the collegiate church; It is also the responsibility of the cathedral chapter to carry out those duties that the law or the diocesan Bishop entrusts to it.

505
505. Every chapter, both cathedral and collegiate, must have its own statutes, prepared by legitimate capitular act and approved by the diocesan Bishop; These statutes cannot be modified or abrogated without the approval of the diocesan Bishop.

506
506. § 1 . The statutes of the chapter, always safeguarding the founding laws, will determine its constitution and the number of canons; They will establish what the chapter and each of the canons must do regarding divine worship and the fulfillment of the ministry; They will regulate the meetings in which the affairs of the council are discussed and, always respecting the prescriptions of universal law, they will establish the conditions required for the validity and legality of the acts.
§ 2 . The statutes will also determine the remuneration, which must be received both in a stable manner and on the occasion of the performance of a function, as well as, in accordance with the norms given by the Holy See, what the insignia of the canons are.

507
507. § 1 . There must be one among the canons who presides over the chapter, and other offices will also be designated in accordance with the statutes, also taking into account current usage in the region.
§ 2 . Other offices may also be entrusted to clerics who do not belong to the chapter, with which these clerics assist the canons according to the statutes.

508
508. § 1 . The penitentiary canon, whether of a cathedral church or a collegiate church, has by virtue of his office the ordinary, non-delegable faculty of absolving in the sacramental forum of latae sententiae censures not declared or reserved to the Holy See, even with respect to those who are in the diocese without belonging to it, and with respect to diocesans, even outside its territory.
§ 2 . Where there is no chapter, the diocesan Bishop will appoint a priest to fulfill this same function.

509
509. § 1 . After hearing the chapter, it is up to the diocesan Bishop, but not the diocesan Administrator, to confer each and every one of the canonries, both in the cathedral church and in a collegiate church, any contrary privilege being revoked; It is also up to the Bishop to confirm whoever has been chosen by the council to preside over it.
§ 2 . The Bishop must confer canonries only on priests who, standing out for their doctrine and integrity of life, have carried out their ministry meritorious.

510
510. § 1 . Parishes must not be united again to a chapter of canons, and parishes that were already united to a chapter must be separated from it by the diocesan Bishop.
§ 2 . In the church that is both parish and capitular, a parish priest must be appointed, whether or not chosen from among the canons; This parish priest has all the duties and all the rights and faculties that, according to the rule of law, are proper to a parish priest.
§ 3 . It is the responsibility of the diocesan Bishop to establish fixed norms with which the pastoral obligations of the parish priest and the functions of the chapter are duly adjusted, ensuring that the parish priest does not hinder the capitular functions nor the chapter the parish functions; If there is any conflict, it will be resolved by the diocesan Bishop, who will first ensure that the pastoral needs of the faithful are duly attended to.
§ 4 . Alms to a church that is both parish and capitular are presumed to have been given to the parish, unless otherwise stated.

511
511. To the extent that pastoral circumstances advise, a pastoral council will be constituted in each diocese, whose responsibility is, under the authority of the Bishop, to study and evaluate what concerns pastoral activities in the diocese, and to suggest practical conclusions about them.

512
512. § 1 . The pastoral council is made up of faithful who are in full communion with the Catholic Church, both clerics and members of institutes of consecrated life and above all lay people, who are appointed according to the manner determined by the diocesan Bishop.
§ 2 . The faithful who are appointed to the pastoral council must be chosen so that through them the portion of the people of God that constitutes the diocese is truly reflected, taking into account their different regions, social and professional conditions, as well as the part they have in the apostolate, both personally and in association with others.
§ 3 . Only faithful who stand out for their secure faith, good morals and prudence should be appointed to the pastoral council.

513
513. § 1 . The pastoral council is constituted for a specific period of time, in accordance with what the statutes given by the Bishop prescribe.
§ 2 . When the seat is vacated, the pastoral council ceases.

514
514. § 1 . It is exclusively the responsibility of the diocesan Bishop, according to the needs of the apostolate, to convene and preside over the pastoral council, which has only advisory votes; It is also solely up to the Bishop to make public what was discussed in the council.
§ 2 . It must be convened at least once a year.

515
515. § 1 The parish is a certain community of faithful constituted in a stable manner in the particular Church, whose pastoral care, under the authority of the diocesan Bishop, is entrusted to a parish priest, as its own pastor.
§ 2 . It is exclusively up to the diocesan Bishop to erect, suppress or change the parishes, but he must not erect, suppress or change them significantly without having heard the presbyteral council.
§ 3 . The legitimately erected parish has legal personality by virtue of the law itself.

516
516. § 1 . Unless the law provides otherwise, the quasi-parish is equated to the parish, which is a specific community of faithful within the particular Church, entrusted, as its own pastor, to a priest, but which, due to peculiar circumstances, has not yet been established as a parish.
§ 2 . When some communities cannot be erected as parishes or quasi-parishes, the diocesan Bishop will provide in another way for the pastoral care of them.

517
517. § 1 . When circumstances so require, the pastoral care of one or more parishes at a time can be entrusted jointly to several priests, provided that one of them is the director of the pastoral care, who directs the joint activity and is responsible for it before the Bishop.
§ 2 . If, due to a shortage of priests, the diocesan Bishop considers that participation in the exercise of the pastoral care of the parish must be entrusted to a deacon or another person who does not have a priestly character, or to a community, he will designate a priest who, endowed with the powers of the parish priest, directs the pastoral activity.
517. § 1, if the pastoral care is entrusted jointly to several.
§ 2 . The entrustment of a parish, which is discussed in § 1, can be carried out both in perpetuity and for a fixed period; In both cases, it will be done through a written agreement between the diocesan Bishop and the competent Superior of the institute or society, in which, among other things, everything that refers to the work to be carried out, the people who will dedicate themselves to it and the financial matters will be expressly and detailedly determined.
517. § 1, the contrary custom being disapproved and all contrary privileges revoked.
517. § 1, the pastoral care of one or several parishes is jointly entrusted:
1 must be endowed with the qualities indicated in c. 521;
2 will be appointed or instituted in accordance with what is prescribed in cc. 522 and 524;
3 take charge of pastoral care only from the moment they take office; The inauguration is granted to its moderator according to the prescriptions of c.
517. § 1, or the moderator thereof, or when one of them becomes incapacitated for the exercise of the pastoral function, this does not mean that the parish or parishes entrusted to the care of the group remain vacant; but it is up to the diocesan Bishop to appoint another moderator and, as long as this is not constituted by the Bishop, the priest of the oldest group by appointment will perform this function.

518
518. As a general rule, the parish must be territorial, that is, it must include all the faithful of a given territory; but, where appropriate, personal parishes will be established based on the rite, language or nationality of the faithful of a territory, or even for another specific reason.

519
519. The parish priest is the proper pastor of the parish entrusted to him, and exercises the pastoral care of the community entrusted to him under the authority of the diocesan Bishop in whose ministry of Christ he has been called to participate, so that in that same community he fulfills the functions of teaching, sanctifying and governing, with the cooperation also of other priests or deacons, and with the help of lay faithful, in accordance with the norm of law.

520
520. § 1 . A parish priest must not be a legal person; but the diocesan Bishop, not the diocesan Administrator, may, with the consent of the competent Superior, entrust a parish to a clerical religious institute or to a clerical society of apostolic life, even erecting the parish in a church of the institute or society, with the condition, however, that a priest be the parish priest of the same or the moderator referred to in the c

521
521. § 1 . In order for someone to be validly appointed as a parish priest, they must have received the sacred orders of the priesthood.
§ 2 . He must also stand out for his sound doctrine and moral probity, be endowed with zeal for souls and other virtues, and have the qualities that are required by both universal and particular law, for the care of the parish in question.
§ 3 . For someone to be appointed to the office of parish priest, it is necessary that his suitability be confirmed with certainty according to the manner established by the diocesan Bishop, including through an examination.

522
522. The parish priest must have stability and therefore must be appointed for an indefinite period; He can only be appointed by the diocesan Bishop for a specific period of time, if this method of procedure has been admitted, by decree, by the Episcopal Conference.

523
523. Subject to the provisions of the c

524
524. The diocesan Bishop must entrust the parish that has become vacant to the one who, considering all the circumstances, he considers suitable to carry out the parish priest there, leaving aside any respect for persons; To judge the suitability, listen to the archpriest and carry out the appropriate investigations, asking for the opinion, if the case warrants it, of some priests and lay faithful.

525
525. When the see is vacant or impeded, it is up to the diocesan Administrator or the person who provisionally governs the diocese:
1 granting institution or confirmation to priests who have been legitimately presented or elected to a parish;
2 appoint parish priests, if a year has already passed since the see became vacant or impeded.

526
526. § 1 . The parish priest must have the care of a single parish; However, due to a shortage of priests or other circumstances, the same priest can be entrusted with the care of several nearby parishes.
§ 2 . In each parish there must be only one pastor or director according to the norm of c.

527
527. § 1 . Whoever has been promoted to carry out the pastoral care of a parish obtains it and is obliged to exercise it from the moment he takes possession.
§ 2 . Possession is granted to the parish priest by the local Ordinary or a priest delegated by him, according to the manner received by particular law or legitimate custom; However, the same Ordinary may, with just cause, dispense from observance in this way, in which case, the notification of the dispensation serves as the taking of possession.
§ 3 . The local Ordinary will determine the time within which possession of the parish must be taken; and, if this passes without effect, lacking a just impediment, he can declare the parish vacant.
527. § 2 and, for other priests, the legitimately issued profession of faith serves as the taking of possession.

528
528. § 1 . The parish priest is obliged to ensure that the word of God is announced in its entirety to those who live in the parish; Take care therefore that the lay faithful are indoctrinated in the truths of the faith, especially through the homily, which must be given on Sundays and holy days of obligation, and catechetical formation; It must encourage initiatives that promote the evangelical spirit, also with regard to social justice; It must particularly seek the Catholic formation of children and young people and strive with all possible means, also with the collaboration of the faithful, so that the evangelical message also reaches those who have stopped practicing or do not profess the true faith.
§ 2 . The parish priest should strive to make the most holy Eucharist the center of the parish community of faithful; work so that the faithful are nourished with the pious celebration of the sacraments, in a peculiar way with the frequent reception of the most holy Eucharist and penance; Try to move them to prayer, also within families, and to conscious and active participation in the sacred liturgy, which, under the authority of the diocesan Bishop, the parish priest must moderate in his parish, with the obligation to monitor so that abuses are not introduced.

529
529. § 1 . To diligently fulfill his pastoral function, the parish priest should seek to know the faithful entrusted to him; To this end, he will visit families, participating in a particular way in the concerns, anguish and pain of the faithful due to the death of loved ones, comforting them in the Lord and prudently correcting them if they deviate from good behavior; He must help the sick with lavish charity, especially the dying, diligently strengthening them with the administration of the sacraments and entrusting their soul to God; It must be dedicated with particular diligence to the poor, the afflicted, those who are alone, emigrants or those who suffer special difficulties; and it must also provide the means so that spouses and parents are helped in the fulfillment of their own duties and Christian life is encouraged within families.
§ 2 . Let the parish priest recognize and promote the proper role of the lay faithful in the mission of the Church, encouraging their associations for religious purposes.
