[Original wording of the canon modified by His Holiness Pope Francis (cf. Letter 
apostolic in the form of Motu proprio 

Communis vita with which some norms of the 
Code of Canon Law, March 19, 2019)]:

705
705. The religious elevated to the episcopate remains a member of his institute, but, by the vow of obedience, he is subject exclusively to the Roman Pontiff, and he is not bound by those duties that he himself prudently judges to be incompatible with his condition.

706
706. The same religious:
1 if due to profession he lost control of the assets, he has the use, usufruct and administration of the assets he subsequently acquires; but, both the diocesan Bishop and those others referred to in c.

707
707. § 1 . The religious retired as a Bishop can also choose his residence outside of a house of his institute, unless the Apostolic See establishes otherwise.
§ 2 . As regards its convenient and dignified support, if it had served a diocese, the provisions of c.

708
708. Major Superiors can usefully associate in conferences or councils, so that, in unity of effort, they may work to more fully achieve the goal of each institute, safeguarding their autonomy, character and spirit, either to deal with common issues or to establish appropriate coordination and cooperation with the Episcopal Conferences, as well as with each of the Bishops.

709
709. The conferences of major Superiors have their own statutes approved by the Holy See, which is solely responsible for establishing them as a legal entity and under whose supreme authority they remain.

710
710. A secular institute is an institute of consecrated life in which the faithful, living in the world, aspire to the perfection of charity, and dedicate themselves to seeking the sanctification of the world, especially from within it.

711
711. By consecration, a member of a secular institute does not modify his or her own canonical, clerical or lay status in the people of God, observing the prescriptions of law relating to institutes of consecrated life.

712
712. Without prejudice to the prescriptions of cc. 598-601, the constitutions must establish the sacred bonds with which the evangelical councils are embraced in the institute, and will determine the obligations that arise from these bonds, preserving however in the way of living the secularity proper to the institute.

713
713. § 1 . The members of these institutes manifest and exercise their own consecration in apostolic activity and, like leaven, they strive to impregnate all things with the evangelical spirit, for the strength and increase of the Body of Christ.
§ 2 . The lay members participate in the evangelizing function of the Church in the world and taking advantage of the world either with the testimony of Christian life and fidelity to their consecration, or with the collaboration they provide to order temporal affairs according to God and inform the world with the force of the Gospel. And they also offer their own cooperation in the service of the ecclesial community, in accordance with their secular way of life.
§ 3 . The clergy members, through the testimony of consecrated life, help above all their brothers in the presbytery with peculiar apostolic charity, and carry out the sanctification of the world among the people of God through their sacred ministry.

714
714. The members must live in the ordinary circumstances of the world, either alone, or with their own family, or in groups of fraternal life, in accordance with the constitutions.

715
715. § 1 . The clerical members incardinated in the diocese depend on the diocesan Bishop, remaining safe with regard to consecrated life in their own institute.
§ 2 . But those who join the institute in accordance with the norm of c.

716
716. § 1 . All members must actively participate in the life of the institute, according to their own law.
§ 2 . The members of the same institute must live in communion with each other, diligently safeguarding unity of spirit and genuine brotherhood.

717
717. § 1 . The constitutions must determine the very mode of regime, the time during which the Directors carry out their office and the way of appointing them.
§ 2 . No one should be appointed General Director if he or she is not permanently incorporated.
§ 3 . Those entrusted with the regime of the institute take care that the unity of spirit is observed and the active participation of the members is encouraged.

718
718. The administration of the assets of the institute, which must manifest and promote evangelical poverty, is governed by the norms of Book V, Of the temporal goods of the Church, as well as by the proper law of the institute. Likewise, the law itself must determine the obligations, especially economic, of the institute with respect to those members who work for it.

719
719. § 1 . So that the members faithfully correspond to their vocation and their apostolic action is the fruit of the same union with Christ, they must dedicate themselves intensely to prayer, read sacred Scripture appropriately, observe the annual times of retreat and perform other exercises of piety according to their own right.
§ 2 . The celebration of the Eucharist, daily as far as possible, should be the source and strength of your entire consecrated life.
§ 3 . They will freely attend the sacrament of penance, which they must receive frequently.
§ 4 . Feel free to have the necessary direction of conscience and seek appropriate advice from your own Directors, if you wish.

720
720. The right to admit to the institute, with regard to both the test and the sacred bonds, whether temporary, perpetual or definitive, corresponds to the Major Directors with their council, in accordance with the constitutions.

721
721. § 1 . He is invalidly admitted to the initial test:
1 who has not yet reached the age of majority;
2 who is linked by sacred bond to an institute of consecrated life or is incorporated into a society of apostolic life;
3 a spouse, during the marriage.
§ 2 . Constitutions may establish other impediments to admission, which even affect validity, or set conditions.
§ 3 . Furthermore, for someone to be received, they must possess the necessary maturity to properly lead the life of the institute.

722
722. § 1 . The initial test should aim for the candidates to better know their divine vocation and that of the institute, and to exercise themselves in the spirit and way of life of the institute.
§ 2 . Candidates must be properly formed to live according to the evangelical counsels and convert their entire lives into an apostolate, using those forms of evangelization that best respond to the purpose, spirit and nature of the institute.
§ 3 . The manner and time of this test prior to the acquisition for the first time of sacred bonds in the institute shall be determined in the constitutions; The duration cannot be less than one biennium.

723
723. § 1 . Once the time of the initial test has elapsed, the candidate who is considered suitable must embrace the three evangelical counsels, corroborated with a sacred bond, or leave the institute.
§ 2 . This first incorporation, not less than five years, must be temporary in accordance with the norm of the constitutions.
§ 3 . Once the time of this incorporation has expired, the member considered suitable will be admitted to incorporation, either perpetually or definitively, that is, with temporary ties that must always be renewed.
§ 4 . Regarding certain legal effects, which must be established in the constitutions, definitive incorporation is equivalent to perpetual incorporation.

724
724. § 1 . After having acquired the sacred bonds for the first time, the formation must continue permanently, according to the constitutions.
§ 2 . The members must be formed at the same time in divine and human things; and the Directors of the institute must diligently take care of the continuous spiritual formation.

725
725. Through some link determined in the constitutions, the institute can associate other faithful who aspire to evangelical perfection according to the spirit of the institute, and participate in its mission.

726
726. § 1 . After the time of temporary incorporation has elapsed, the member may freely leave the institute, or the Senior Director, after hearing his advice and with just cause, may exclude him from the renewal of the sacred bonds.
§ 2 . The temporarily incorporated member who requests it of his own will, may, with serious cause, obtain from the Director General, with the consent of his council, a pardon to leave the institute.

727
727. § 1 . The perpetually incorporated member who wishes to leave the institute, after considering the matter seriously in the presence of God, may ask the Apostolic See, through the Director General, for the necessary indult if the institute is of pontifical right; Otherwise, also to the diocesan Bishop, as determined in the constitutions.
§ 2 . If it is a cleric incardinated to the institute, what is prescribed in c. 693.

728
728. Once the pardon to leave the institute is legitimately granted, all ties cease, as well as the rights and obligations arising from incorporation.

729
729. n The expulsion of a member from the institute is carried out in accordance with 
what is established in cann. 694 § 1, 1 and 2 and 695; the constitutions 
They will also determine other causes of expulsion, as long as they are 
proportionally serious, external, imputable and legally 
verified, proceeding in accordance with the provisions of the cann. 
697-700. The provisions of Can. 701 apply to expulsion.
729. The expulsion of a member of the institute is carried out in accordance with the provisions of the cc

730
730. For the transition of a member from a secular institute to another secular institute, the prescriptions of the ccs must be observed.

731
731. § 1 . Institutes of consecrated life are similar to societies of apostolic life, whose members, without religious vows, seek the apostolic goal proper to the society and, leading fraternal life in common, according to their own way of life, aspire to the perfection of charity through the observance of the constitutions.
§ 2 . Among these there are societies whose members embrace evangelical counsels through a bond determined by the constitutions.
731. § 2, ccs also apply. 598-602.

732
732. The provisions of cc. 578-597 and 606, however, leaving aside the nature of each society; but to the companies mentioned in the c

733
733. § 1 . The competent authority of the society erects the house and constitutes the local community with the prior consent, given in writing, of the diocesan Bishop, who must also be consulted for its suppression.
§ 2 . The consent to erect a house entails the right to have at least an oratory, in which the Most Holy Eucharist is celebrated and reserved.

734
734. The government of society is determined in the constitutions, and ccs will be observed. 617-633, respecting the nature of each society.

735
735. § 1 . The admission of members, their testing, incorporation and training are determined by the law of each society.
§ 2 . Regarding admission into a company, the conditions established in ccs must be observed. 642-645.
§ 3 . The proper law must determine the mode of testing and training adapted to the purpose and character of the society, especially doctrinal, spiritual and apostolic, so that the members, in accordance with their divine vocation, are adequately prepared for the mission and life of the society.

736
736. § 1 . In clerical societies, the clerics are incorporated into the same society, unless the constitutions provide otherwise.
§ 2 . Regarding the curriculum and the reception of orders, the norms prescribed for secular clergy must be observed, leaving aside what is established by § 1.

737
737. On the part of the members, incorporation carries with it the obligations and rights determined by the constitutions; and, on the part of society, the request to guide its members towards the end of their own vocation, in accordance with the constitutions.

738
738. § 1 . All members are subject to their own Moderators, in accordance with the norm of the constitutions, with regard to the internal life and discipline of the society.
§ 2 . They are also subject to the diocesan Bishop with regard to public worship, the care of souls and other works of apostolate, taking into account the ccs. 679-683.
§ 3 . The relations of a member incardinated in a diocese with its own Bishop are determined by the constitutions or by particular agreements.

739
739. In addition to the obligations that correspond to them according to the constitutions, the members have the common duties of clerics, unless, due to the very nature of the thing or the context, otherwise is established.

740
740. The members must live in the house or in the legitimately constituted community, and lead a common life, in accordance with their own law, by which absences from the house or the community are also governed.

741
741. § 1 . The societies and, if the constitutions do not determine otherwise, their constituencies and houses, are legal persons and, as such, have the capacity to acquire, possess, manage and dispose of temporal goods, in accordance with the prescriptions of Book V, Of the temporal goods of the Church, of ccs. 636,

742
742. The departure and expulsion of a member not yet definitively incorporated is governed by the constitutions of each society.

743
743. Without prejudice to what is prescribed in c. 693, the pardon to leave the society, with the cessation of the rights and obligations arising from the incorporation, may be granted to a member permanently incorporated by the Supreme Moderator with the consent of his council, unless according to the constitutions it is reserved to the Holy See.

744
744. § 1 . It is also reserved to the Supreme Moderator, with the consent of his council, to grant permission for a definitively incorporated member to move to another society of apostolic life, while the rights and obligations in the society itself remain suspended, but without prejudice to the right to return to it before definitive incorporation into the new society.
§ 2 . For the transition from an institute of consecrated life to a society of apostolic life, or vice versa, a license from the Holy See is required, whose mandates must be adhered to.

745
745. The Supreme Moderator, with the consent of his council, may grant a pardon to a permanently incorporated member to live outside the society, but not for more than three years, leaving in suspension the rights and obligations not compatible with his new condition; but it remains, however, under the care of the Moderators. If it is a cleric, the consent of the Ordinary of the place where he must reside is also required, under whose care and dependence he also remains.

746
746. For the expulsion of a permanently incorporated member, ccs. 694-704.

747
747. § 1 . The Church, to which Christ Our Lord entrusted the deposit of faith, so that, with the assistance of the Holy Spirit, it would holyly guard the revealed truth, delve into it and announce and expound it faithfully, has the duty and the original right, independent of any human power, to preach the Gospel to all people, even using its own means of social communication.
§ 2 . It is the responsibility of the Church always and everywhere to proclaim moral principles, including those referring to the social order, as well as to give its judgment on any human affairs, to the extent that the fundamental rights of the human person or the salvation of souls require it.

748
748. § 1 . All men are obliged to seek the truth in that which refers to God and his Church and, once known, they have, by divine law, the duty and the right to embrace and observe it.
§ 2 . It is never permissible for anyone to coerce men to embrace the Catholic faith against their own conscience.

749
749. § 1 . By virtue of his office, the Supreme Pontiff enjoys infallibility in the teaching profession, when, as Supreme Pastor and Doctor of all the faithful, who is responsible for confirming his brothers in the faith, he proclaims by a definitive act the doctrine that must be upheld in matters of faith and customs.
§ 2 . The College of Bishops also has infallibility in the magisterium when the Bishops exercise such magisterium gathered in the Ecumenical Council and, as doctors and judges of faith and customs, declare for the entire Church that a doctrine on faith or customs must be held as definitive; or when dispersed throughout the world but maintaining the bond of communion among themselves and with the successor of Peter, teaching in an authentic way together with the Roman Pontiff himself the matters of faith and customs, they agree that an opinion must be held as definitive.
§ 3 . No doctrine is considered infallibly defined if it is not manifestly defined.

750
750. n § 1 . Everything that is contained in the word of God written or transmitted by tradition, that is, in the only deposit of faith entrusted to the Church, and which is also proposed as revealed by God, either by the solemn magisterium of the Church, or by its ordinary and universal magisterium, which is manifested in the common adhesion of the faithful under the guidance of the sacred magisterium, must be believed with divine and catholic faith; Therefore, everyone is obliged to avoid any contrary doctrine.
§ 2 . Likewise, each and every one of the things regarding the doctrine of faith and customs definitively proposed by the Magisterium of the Church must be firmly accepted and retained, namely, those that are necessary to holyly guard and faithfully expose the very deposit of faith; Therefore, he opposes the doctrine of the Catholic Church who rejects said propositions that must be retained definitively.
750. Everything that is contained in the word of God written or transmitted by tradition, that is, in the only deposit of faith entrusted to the Church, and which is also proposed as revealed by God, either by the solemn magisterium of the Church, or by its ordinary and universal magisterium, which is manifested in the common adhesion of the faithful under the guidance of the sacred magisterium, must be believed with divine and catholic faith; Therefore, everyone is obliged to avoid any contrary doctrine.

751
751. The persistent denial, after receiving baptism, of a truth that must be believed with divine and Catholic faith, or the persistent doubt about it, is called heresy; apostasy is the total rejection of the Christian faith; schism, the rejection of subjection to the Supreme Pontiff or of communion with the members of the Church subject to him.

752
752. A religious assent of the understanding and will, without it becoming faith, must be given to the doctrine that the Supreme Pontiff or the College of Bishops, in the exercise of their authentic magisterium, teach about faith and customs, even if it is not their intention to proclaim it with a decision-making act; Therefore, the faithful take care to avoid everything that is not consistent with it.

753
753. The Bishops who are in communion with the Head and the members of the College, both individually and gathered in Episcopal Conferences or in particular councils, although they are not infallible in their teaching, are authentic doctors and teachers of the faithful entrusted to their care, and the faithful are obliged to adhere with religious assent to this authentic magisterium of their Bishops.

754
754. All the faithful are obliged to observe the constitutions and decrees promulgated by the legitimate authority of the Church to propose doctrine and reject erroneous opinions, and especially those promulgated by the Roman Pontiff or the College of Bishops.

755
755. § 1 . It is first of all the responsibility of the entire College of Bishops and the Apostolic See to promote and direct among Catholics the ecumenical movement, whose purpose is to reintegrate all Christians into unity, a unity that the Church, by the will of Christ, is obliged to promote.
§ 2 . It is also the responsibility of the Bishops and, in accordance with the rule of law, of the Episcopal Conferences, to promote the same unity and, according to the need or convenience of the moment, to establish practical norms, taking into account the prescriptions dictated by the supreme authority of the Church.
(n Indicates that the text corresponds to the new version)
[Original wording of the canon modified by His Holiness Pope John Paul II (cf. Apostolic Letter in the form of Motu Proprio Ad Tuendam Fidem of May 18, 1998)]:

756
756. § 1 . Regarding the universal Church, the function of announcing the Gospel has been entrusted mainly to the Roman Pontiff and the Episcopal College.
§ 2 . In relation to the particular Church that has been entrusted to him, each Bishop exercises this function, who is certainly the moderator of the entire ministry of the word; Sometimes, however, some Bishops jointly exercise that function for several Churches, according to the rule of law.

757
757. It is proper for priests, as cooperators of the Bishops, to announce the Gospel of God; This obligation mainly affects, with respect to the people entrusted to them, the parish priests and those others to whom the care of souls is entrusted; It is also up to the deacons to serve in the ministry of the word to the people of God, in communion with the Bishop and his presbytery.

758
758. The members of institutes of consecrated life, by virtue of their own consecration to God, bear witness to the Gospel in a peculiar way, and are appropriately assumed by the Bishop as help to announce the Gospel.

759
759. By virtue of baptism and confirmation, the lay faithful are witnesses of the evangelical proclamation with their word and the example of their Christian life; They may also be called to cooperate with the Bishop and with the priests in the exercise of the ministry of the word.

760
760. The mystery of Christ must be proposed completely and faithfully in the ministry of the word, which must be founded on sacred Scripture, Tradition, the liturgy, the teaching and the life of the Church.

761
761. All available means must be used to announce Christian doctrine, especially preaching and catechesis, which always occupy a primary place; but also the teaching of the doctrine in schools, academies, conferences and meetings of all kinds, as well as its dissemination through public statements, made by the legitimate authority on the occasion of certain events through the press and other means of social communication.

762
762. Since the people of God are gathered above all by the word of the living God, which there is an absolute right to demand from the lips of priests, the sacred ministers must have a high regard for the function of preaching, among whose main duties is that of announcing the Gospel of God to all.

763
763. Bishops have the right to preach the word of God in any place, without excluding the churches and oratories of religious institutes of pontifical right, unless, in particular cases, the local Bishop expressly objects.

764
764. Keeping in mind what is prescribed in c. 765, priests and deacons have the power to preach everywhere, which they must exercise with the at least presumed consent of the rector of the church, unless this power has been restricted or taken away from them by the competent Ordinary, or unless express license is required by particular law.

765
765. To preach to religious in their churches or oratories, a license from the competent Superior is required in accordance with the constitutions.

766
766. Lay people may be admitted to preach in a church or oratory, if in certain circumstances there is a need for it, or if, in particular cases, utility advises it, according to the prescriptions of the Episcopal Conference and without prejudice to the c

767
767. § 1 .
767. § 1 . Among the forms of preaching, the homily stands out, which is part of the liturgy itself and is reserved for the priest or deacon; throughout the liturgical year, expose yourselves in it, starting from the sacred text, the mysteries of the faith and the norms of Christian life.
§ 2 . In all Sunday Masses and holy days of obligation that are celebrated with the participation of the people, there must be a homily, and it cannot be omitted without serious cause.
§ 3 . It is highly advisable that, if there is enough participation from the people, there is also a homily at the Masses celebrated during the week, especially during the season of Advent and Lent, or on the occasion of a festival or a mourning event.
§ 4 . It is up to the parish priest or rector of the church to ensure that these prescriptions are faithfully fulfilled.

768
768. § 1 . The preachers of the word of God propose to the faithful first of all what is necessary to believe and do for the glory of God and salvation of men.
§ 2 . They should also teach the faithful the doctrine proposed by the Magisterium of the Church on the dignity and freedom of the human person; about the unity, stability and duties of the family; about the obligations that correspond to men united in society; and on the way of arranging temporal affairs according to the order established by God.

769
769. Christian doctrine should be proposed in a manner adapted to the condition of the listeners and adapted to the needs of each era.

770
770. In certain periods, according to the prescriptions of the diocesan Bishop, parish priests organize those forms of preaching called spiritual exercises and sacred missions, or others adapted to the needs.

771
771. § 1 . Pastors of souls, especially Bishops and parish priests, should be anxious that the word of God be announced also to those faithful who, due to their living conditions, do not sufficiently enjoy the common and ordinary pastoral care, or who lack it completely.
§ 2 . They also ensure that the message of the Gospel reaches the non-believers who live in the territory, since the healing of souls must also reach these, as well as the faithful.

772
772. § 1 . Regarding the exercise of preaching, everyone also observes the prescriptions established by the diocesan Bishop.
§ 2 . To speak on topics of Christian doctrine on radio or television, the prescriptions established by the Episcopal Conference must be met.

773
773. It is a proper and serious duty, especially of the shepherds of souls, to take care of the catechesis of the Christian people, so that the faith of the faithful, through the teaching of the doctrine and the practice of the Christian life, becomes alive, explicit and operational.

774
774. § 1 . The request for catechesis, under the direction of the legitimate ecclesiastical authority, corresponds to all members of the Church to the extent of each one.
§ 2 . Before anyone else, parents are obliged to form their children in the faith and practice of the Christian life, through word and example; and those who act as parents and godparents have a similar obligation.
774. § 2 .

775
775. § 1 . Observing the prescriptions of the Apostolic See, it is up to the diocesan Bishop to dictate norms on catechesis and ensure that adequate instruments are available for it, even developing a catechism, if it seems appropriate; as well as promoting and coordinating catechetical initiatives.
§ 2 . n It is the responsibility of the Episcopal Conference, if it is considered useful, to ensure the edition of catechisms for its territory, after confirmation from the Apostolic See.
§ 3 . Within the Episcopal Conference, a catechetical department can be established, whose main task will be to help each diocese in matters of catechesis.
775. § 2 . It is the responsibility of the Episcopal Conference, if it is considered useful, to ensure the edition of catechisms for its territory, with prior approval from the Apostolic See.
775. § 2, the approval of the local Ordinary is necessary to edit catechisms and other writings related to catechetical formation, as well as their translations.
§ 2 . In schools, whether elementary, middle or higher, those books that deal with issues relating to sacred Scripture, theology, canon law, ecclesiastical history and religious or moral subjects that have not been published with the approval of the competent ecclesiastical authority, or have subsequently obtained it, cannot be used as textbooks for teaching.
§ 3 . It is recommended that books on the subjects referred to in § 2 be submitted to the judgment of the local Ordinary, even if they are not used as textbooks in teaching, and also those writings that contain something that peculiarly affects religion or the integrity of customs.
§ 4 . In churches and oratories, books or other writings that deal with questions of religion or customs that have not been published with license from the competent ecclesiastical authority, or subsequently approved by it, cannot be displayed, sold or given.

776
776. By virtue of his office, the parish priest must take care of the catechetical formation of adults, young people and children, for which he will use the collaboration of the clerics assigned to the parish, the members of institutes of consecrated life and societies of apostolic life, taking into account the nature of each institute, and also of the lay faithful, especially catechists; All of these, if they are not legitimately prevented, do not refuse to give their help willingly. Promote and encourage the duty of parents in the family catechesis referred to in the c

777
777. The parish priest should especially ensure, taking into account the norms dictated by the diocesan Bishop:
1 that an appropriate catechesis be taught for the celebration of the sacraments;
2 that children prepare well to receive for the first time the sacraments of penance, the most holy Eucharist and confirmation, through a catechesis taught during the time that is convenient;
3 that they, after their first communion, be educated with a broader and deeper catechetical formation;
4 that, to the extent permitted by their own condition, catechetical training is also given to the physically or mentally handicapped;
5 that, through various forms and activities, the faith of young people and adults is strengthened, illustrated and developed.

778
778. Religious Superiors and those of societies of apostolic life should ensure that catechetical formation is diligently taught in their churches, schools and other works that have been entrusted to them in any way.

779
779. Catechetical formation must be given using all those means, teaching materials and instruments of social communication that are most effective so that the faithful, in a manner adapted to their way of being, ability, age and living conditions, can learn Catholic doctrine more completely and put it better into practice.

780
780. The local Ordinaries should ensure that the catechists are properly prepared to carry out their task well, that is, that they are given ongoing formation, and that they themselves know the doctrine of the Church well and learn theoretically and practically the norms of the pedagogical disciplines.
(n Indicates that the text corresponds to the new version)
[Original wording of the canons modified by His Holiness Pope Francis (cf. Apostolic Letter in the form of Motu Proprio Competentias quasdam decernere of February 11, 2022)]:

781
781. Since, by its very nature, the entire Church is missionary, and the task of evangelization is the fundamental duty of the people of God, all the faithful, aware of their own responsibility, assume their part in missionary activity.

782
782. § 1 . The supreme direction and coordination of the initiatives and activities that refer to missionary work and missionary cooperation corresponds to the Roman Pontiff and the College of Bishops.
§ 2 . Each Bishop, insofar as he is responsible for the universal Church and all the Churches, shows a peculiar concern for the missionary task, above all by raising, promoting and sustaining missionary initiatives in his own particular Church.

783
783. Since by their very consecration they dedicate themselves to the service of the Church, the members of the institutes of consecrated life are obliged to contribute in a special way to the missionary task, according to the proper way of their institute.

784
784. Missionaries, that is, those who are sent by the competent ecclesiastical authority to carry out missionary work, may or may not be chosen from among the natives, whether they are secular clerics, members of institutes of consecrated life or of a society of apostolic life, or other lay faithful.

785
785. § 1 . To carry out the missionary task, catechists must be used, that is, lay faithful who are duly instructed and stand out for their Christian life, who, under the direction of a missionary, dedicate themselves to explaining evangelical doctrine and organizing liturgical acts and works of charity.
§ 2 . Catechists must be trained in schools designed for this purpose or, where there are none, under the direction of missionaries.

786
786. The missionary activity itself, through which the Church is established in towns or groups in which it is not yet rooted, is carried out by the Church mainly by sending preachers until the new Churches are fully constituted, that is, when they are provided with their own forces and sufficient means to be able to carry out the task of evangelizing on their own.

787
787. § 1 . With the testimony of their life and their word, missionaries should establish a sincere dialogue with those who do not believe in Christ, so that, in a manner adapted to their mentality and culture, they can open the paths by which they can be led to know the evangelical message.
§ 2 . Take care to teach the truths of the faith to those who you consider prepared to receive the evangelical message, so that, by freely asking for it, they can be admitted to the reception of baptism.

788
788. § 1 . Those who have expressed their willingness to embrace faith in Christ, once the time of precatechumenate has expired, are admitted in liturgical ceremonies to the catechumenate, and their names are inscribed in a book intended for this purpose.
§ 2 . Through the teaching and learning of the Christian life, catechumens must be properly initiated into the mystery of salvation, and introduced to the life of faith, liturgy and charity of the people of God, and of the apostolate.
§ 3 . It is up to the Episcopal Conferences to publish statutes that regulate the catechumenate, determining what obligations catechumens must fulfill and what prerogatives are recognized.

789
789. Neophytes should be trained with appropriate teaching so that they know more deeply the evangelical truth and the obligations that, through baptism, they have assumed and must fulfill; and a sincere love for Christ and his Church will be instilled in them.

790
790. § 1 . In the mission territories, the diocesan Bishop is responsible for:
1 promote, direct and coordinate initiatives and works that refer to missionary activity;
2 take care that the appropriate agreements are made with the Moderators of the institutes that are dedicated to missionary work, and that relations with them benefit the mission.
§ 2 . All missionaries, including religious and their auxiliaries who reside within the Bishop's demarcation, are subject to the prescriptions of the diocesan Bishop indicated in § 1, 1.

791
791. In all dioceses, to promote missionary cooperation:
1 foster missionary vocations;
2 a priest be assigned to effectively promote initiatives in favor of the missions, especially the Pontifical Mission Societies;
3 celebrate the annual day in favor of the missions;
4 A proportionate quota is paid each year for the missions, which will be remitted to the Holy See.

792
792. Episcopal Conferences must create and promote institutions that fraternally welcome and help with appropriate pastoral care to those who, for reasons of work or study, come to their territory from mission lands.

793
793. § 1 . Parents and those who take their place have the obligation and the right to educate their children; Catholic parents also have the obligation and the right to choose those means and institutions through which, according to the circumstances of each place, they can best provide for the Catholic education of their children.
§ 2 . Parents also have the right to have civil society provide them with the help they need to provide their children with a Catholic education.

794
794. § 1 . In a singular way, the duty and right to educate belongs to the Church, to whom God has entrusted the mission of helping men so that they can reach the fullness of the Christian life.
§ 2 . Pastors of souls have the duty to provide what is necessary so that all the faithful receive a Catholic education.

795
795. Since true education must seek the integral formation of the human person, in order to its ultimate goal and, simultaneously, to the common good of society, children and young people must be educated so that they can harmoniously develop their physical, moral and intellectual gifts, acquire a more perfect sense of responsibility and a correct use of freedom, and prepare to participate actively in social life.

796
796. § 1 . Among the means to achieve education, the faithful highly value schools, which constitute a primary help for parents in fulfilling their duty to educate.
§ 2 . It is necessary for parents to cooperate closely with the teachers of the schools to which they entrust the education of their children; The teachers, in turn, in fulfilling their duties, must work closely with the parents, to whom they must listen willingly, and whose associations or meetings must be organized and highly appreciated.

797
797. It is necessary that parents have true freedom to choose schools; Therefore, the faithful must be solicitous so that civil society recognizes this parental freedom and, in accordance with distributive justice, also protects it with economic aid.

798
798. Parents must entrust their children to those schools in which a Catholic education is taught; But, if this is not possible, they have the obligation to ensure that proper Catholic education is organized outside the schools.

799
799. The faithful must strive so that, in civil society, the laws that regulate the training of young people also provide for their religious and moral education in the same schools, according to the conscience of their parents.

800
800. § 1 . The Church has the right to establish and direct schools of any subject, gender and grade.
§2 . Let the faithful promote Catholic schools, helping to the extent of their strength to create and sustain them.

801
801. Religious institutes whose mission is teaching, remaining faithful to this mission of theirs, endeavor to dedicate themselves to Catholic education also through their schools, established with the consent of the diocesan Bishop.

802
802. § 1 . If there are no schools in which an education imbued with the Christian spirit is taught, it is up to the diocesan Bishop to ensure their creation.
§ 2 . Where appropriate, the diocesan Bishop also provides for the creation of professional and technical schools, and others that are required due to special needs.

803
803. § 1 . A Catholic school is understood to be one that is run by the competent ecclesiastical authority or a public ecclesiastical legal entity, or that the ecclesiastical authority recognizes as such by written document.
§ 2 . Teaching and education in a Catholic school must be based on the principles of Catholic doctrine; and teachers must stand out for their right doctrine and integrity of life.
§ 3 . No school, even if it is actually Catholic, can adopt the name "Catholic school" without the consent of the competent ecclesiastical authority.

804
804. § 1 . The Catholic religious teaching and education that is taught in any schools or carried out in the various media of social communication depends on the authority of the Church; It is up to the Episcopal Conference to give general regulations on this activity, and it is up to the diocesan Bishop to organize it and exercise oversight over it.
§ 2 . The local Ordinary should ensure that the teachers who are assigned to teach religion in schools, even in non-Catholic ones, stand out for their correct doctrine, for the testimony of their Christian life and for their pedagogical aptitude.

805
805. The local Ordinary, within his diocese, has the right to appoint or approve teachers of religion, as well as to remove or demand that they be removed when a religious or moral reason requires it.

806
806. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop has the right to supervise and visit Catholic schools established in his territory, even those founded or directed by members of religious institutes; It is also responsible for dictating regulations on the general organization of Catholic schools; Such rules are also valid for schools run by members of these institutes, without prejudice to their autonomy with regard to the internal regime of these schools.
§ 2 . Under the supervision of the local Ordinary, the Moderators of Catholic schools must ensure that the formation given in them is, from a scientific point of view, of at least the same category as in other schools in the region.

807
807. The Church has the right to establish and direct universities that contribute to the increase of higher culture and a fuller promotion of the human person, as well as to the fulfillment of the teaching function of the Church itself.

808
808. No university, even if it is in fact Catholic, uses the title or name of "Catholic university" without the consent of the competent ecclesiastical authority.

809
809. Episcopal Conferences should ensure that, if possible and convenient, there are universities or at least faculties adequately distributed in their territory, in which, with respect for their scientific autonomy, the different disciplines are researched and taught in accordance with Catholic doctrine.

810
810. § 1 . The competent authority according to the statutes must ensure that, in Catholic universities, professors are appointed who stand out, not only for their scientific and pedagogical suitability, but also for the rectitude of their doctrine and integrity of life; and that, when such requirements are lacking, they are removed from their position, observing the procedure provided for in the statutes.
§ 2 The Episcopal Conferences and the interested diocesan Bishops have the duty and the right to ensure that the principles of Catholic doctrine are faithfully observed in these universities.

811
811. § 1 . The competent ecclesiastical authority should ensure that in Catholic universities a faculty, an institute or, at least, a chair of theology is erected in which classes are also given to lay students.
§ 2 . In Catholic universities there must be classes in which theological questions that are in connection with the subjects of their faculties are dealt with above all.

812
812. Those who explain theological disciplines in any institute of higher learning must have a mandate from the competent ecclesiastical authority.
812. and 813 about Catholic universities apply equally to ecclesiastical universities and faculties.

813
813. The diocesan Bishop must provide intense pastoral care for the students, even establishing a parish, or, at least, through priests permanently assigned to this task; and ensure that in universities, even non-Catholic ones, there are Catholic university centers that provide help, especially spiritual, to youth.

814
814. What is prescribed for universities applies equally to other institutes of higher learning.

815
815. By virtue of its duty to announce the revealed truth, the universities and ecclesiastical faculties ordered to the investigation of sacred disciplines or those others related to them, and to the scientific instruction of students in these subjects, are specific to the Church.

816
816. § 1 . Universities and ecclesiastical faculties can only be established by erection of the Apostolic See or with approval granted by it; It is also responsible for their supreme direction.
§ 2 . All universities and ecclesiastical faculties must have their own statutes and curriculum approved by the Apostolic See.

817
817. No university or faculty that has not been erected or approved by the Apostolic See can grant academic degrees that have canonical effects in the Church.

818
818. The prescriptions of cc. 810,

819
819. To the extent that the good of a diocese or a religious institute, or even of the universal Church, requires it, the diocesan Bishops or the competent Superiors of the institutes must send to the universities or ecclesiastical faculties young people, clerics and members of the institutes who stand out for their character, virtue and talent.

820
820. The Moderators and professors of the universities and ecclesiastical faculties should ensure that the various faculties of the university collaborate mutually, as far as the subject allows, and that this collaboration also occurs between the university or faculty itself and the other universities or faculties, including non-ecclesiastical ones, so that common work contributes to the better progress of science through conferences, coordinated research programs and other means.

821
821. Let the Episcopal Conference and the diocesan Bishop ensure that, as far as possible, higher institutes of religious sciences are created in which theological disciplines and those others that belong to Christian culture are taught.

822
822. § 1 . The pastors of the Church, in use of a right of the Church and in compliance with their duty, try to use the media of social communication.
§ 2 . The pastors themselves should ensure that the faithful are instructed about their duty to cooperate so that the use of the instruments of social communication is enlivened by the human and Christian spirit.
§ 3 . All the faithful, especially those who in some way participate in the organization or use of these means, must be solicitous in providing support to pastoral activity, so that the Church effectively carries out its mission, also through these means.

823
823. § 1 . To preserve the integrity of the truths of faith and customs, the pastors of the Church have the duty and the right to ensure that neither writings nor the use of social media damage the faith and customs of the Christian faithful; Likewise, to demand that the faithful submit to their judgment the writings that they are going to publish and that are related to faith or customs; and also to reprove writings harmful to the rectitude of the faith or to good customs.
§ 2 . The duty and right discussed in the § 1 correspond to the Bishops, both individually and gathered in particular councils or Episcopal Conferences, with respect to the faithful entrusted to them; and to the supreme authority of the Church with respect to all the people of God.

824
824. § 1 . Unless otherwise established, the local Ordinary whose license or approval must be requested according to the canons of this title to publish books, is the local Ordinary of the author or the Ordinary of the place where the books are published.
§ 2 . What is established in this title about books must be applied to any writings intended for public dissemination, unless otherwise stated.

825
825. § 1 . The books of sacred Scripture can only be published if they have been approved by the Apostolic See or by the Episcopal Conference; Likewise, in order for translations to be edited into the vernacular language, they must have been approved by the same authority and must be accompanied by the necessary and sufficient explanatory notes.
§ 2 . With license from the Episcopal Conference, the Catholic faithful can prepare and publish, also in collaboration with separated brothers, translations of the sacred Scripture accompanied by the appropriate explanatory notes.

826
826. § 1 . As regards liturgical books, observe the prescriptions of c. 838.
§ 2 . To republish liturgical books or parts thereof, as well as their translations into the vernacular language, it is necessary to confirm their conformity with the approved edition, through testimony from the Ordinary of the place where they are published.
§ 3 . Prayer books for public or private use of the faithful are not to be published without permission from the local Ordinary.

827
827. § 1 . Without prejudice to what is prescribed by the c

828
828. It is not permitted to republish collections of decrees or acts published by an ecclesiastical authority without having previously obtained a license from the same authority, and observing the conditions imposed by it.

829
829. The approval or license to edit a work is valid for the original text, but not for successive editions or translations of it.

830
830. § 1 . Respecting the right of each local Ordinary to entrust judgment on the books to people whom he himself has approved, the Episcopal Conference can draw up a list of censors, who stand out for their science, correct doctrine and prudence and are at the disposal of the diocesan curias, or also constitute a commission of censors, which the local Ordinaries can consult.
§ 2 . When carrying out his duty, leaving aside all respect for persons, the censor should keep in mind only the doctrine of the Church on faith and customs, as proposed by the ecclesiastical magisterium.
§ 3 . The censor must give his opinion in writing; and if this is favorable, the Ordinary will grant, according to his prudent judgment, the license for the edition, mentioning his own name, as well as the date and place of granting the license; If he does not grant it, the Ordinary communicates to the author of the work the reasons for the refusal.

831
831. § 1 . Without just and reasonable cause, the faithful should not write anything in newspapers, pamphlets or magazines that clearly tend to attack the Catholic religion or good morals; Clergy and members of religious institutes can only do so with permission from the local Ordinary.
§ 2 . It is up to the Episcopal Conferences to set standards regarding the necessary requirements for clerics or members of religious institutes to take part in radio or television broadcasts that deal with issues relating to Catholic doctrine or customs.

832
832. Members of religious institutes also need permission from their major Superior, in accordance with the norm of the constitutions, to publish writings that refer to questions of religion or customs.

833
833. They have the obligation to personally issue the profession of faith, according to the formula approved by the Apostolic See:
1. before the president or his delegate all those who take part, with a deliberative or consultative vote, in an Ecumenical or particular Council, synod of Bishops and diocesan synod; and the president before the Council or synod;
2. those who have been promoted to the dignity of cardinal, according to the statutes of the sacred College;
3. before the delegate from the Apostolic See, all those who have been promoted to the episcopate, and also those who are equal to the diocesan Bishop;
4. the diocesan Administrator, before the college of consultants;
5. the Vicars General, Episcopal Vicars and Judicial Vicars, before the diocesan Bishop or his delegate;
6. the parish priests, the rector and the professors of theology and philosophy in the seminaries, when they begin to exercise their position, before the local Ordinary or a delegate of his; also those who are going to receive the order of the diaconate;
7. the rector of an ecclesiastical or Catholic university, when he begins to exercise his office, before the Grand Chancellor or, failing that, before the local Ordinary or before their delegates; professors who give classes on subjects related to faith or customs in any university, when they begin to exercise their position, before the rector, if he is a priest, or before the local Ordinary or before his delegates;
8. Superiors in religious institutes and clerical societies of apostolic life, according to the norm of the constitutions.

834
834. § 1 . The Church fulfills the function of sanctifying in a peculiar way through the sacred liturgy, which is rightly considered as the exercise of the priestly function of Jesus Christ, in which the sanctification of men is signified by sensible signs and is carried out according to the manner proper to each one of them, at the same time that the public worship of God is exercised in full by the mystical Body of Jesus Christ, that is, the Head and the members.
§ 2 . This worship is paid when it is offered in the name of the Church by legitimately designated persons and through acts approved by the authority of the Church.

835
835. § 1 . Firstly, the function of sanctifying is exercised by the Bishops, who, having the fullness of the priesthood, are the main dispensers of the mysteries of God and, in the Church entrusted to them, the moderators, promoters and custodians of all liturgical life.
§ 2 . It is also exercised by priests, who, participating in the priesthood of Christ, as his ministers, consecrate themselves to the celebration of divine worship and the sanctification of the people under the authority of the Bishop.
§ 3 . In the celebration of divine worship, deacons act according to the provisions of law.
§ 4 . The other faithful also have their own part in the function of sanctifying, actively participating, according to their own way, in the liturgical celebrations and especially in the Eucharist; Parents participate in the same function in a peculiar way, imbuing married life with the Christian spirit and seeking the Christian education of their children.

836
836. Since Christian worship, in which the common priesthood of the faithful is exercised, is a work that comes from faith and is based on it, sacred ministers must diligently seek to arouse and illustrate faith, especially with the ministry of the word, by which faith is born and nourished.

837
837. § 1 . Liturgical actions are not private actions, but celebrations of the Church itself, which is a "sacrament of unity", that is, a holy people gathered and ordered under the guidance of the Bishops; Therefore, they belong to the entire body of the Church, they manifest it and realize it; but they affect each of its members differently, according to the diversity of orders, functions and current participation.
§ 2 . Liturgical actions, to the extent that their very nature calls for a community celebration and where this can be done, will be carried out with the assistance and active participation of the faithful.
[*] 838 § 1 . The ordination of the sacred liturgy depends exclusively on the authority of the Church, which resides in the Apostolic See and, according to the norms of law, in the diocesan Bishop.
§ 2 . It is the responsibility of the Apostolic See to order the sacred liturgy of the universal Church, edit the liturgical books, review their translations into vernacular languages ​​and ensure that the liturgical norms are faithfully fulfilled everywhere.
§ 3 . It is up to the Episcopal Conferences to prepare the translations of the liturgical books into the vernacular languages, adapting them appropriately within the limits established in the liturgical books themselves, and to edit them with the prior review of the Holy See.
§ 4 . The diocesan Bishop, in the Church entrusted to him and within the limits of his competence, is responsible for giving mandatory norms for all on liturgical matters.

838
838. Codicis Iuris Canonici immutantur (September 3, 2017).
838. §§ 3 and 4, it is necessary to establish what refers to its celebration, administration and lawful reception, as well as the ritual that must be observed in its celebration.

839
839. § 1 . The Church also carries out the function of sanctifying by other means, already with prayers, by which it prays to God that the faithful be sanctified in the truth; already with works of penance and charity, which contribute greatly to the Kingdom of Christ being rooted and strengthened in souls, and also cooperating in the salvation of the world.
§ 2 . The local Ordinaries should ensure that the prayers and pious and sacred practices of the Christian people are in full conformity with the norms of the Church.

[*] For the modifications of this canon cf. Litterae Apostolicae Motu Proprio datae Quibus nonnulla in can.

840
840. The sacraments of the New Testament, instituted by Christ Our Lord and entrusted to the Church, insofar as they are actions of Christ and the Church, are signs and means with which faith is expressed and strengthened, God is worshiped and the sanctification of men is carried out, and therefore contribute greatly to creating, corroborating and manifesting ecclesiastical communion; For this reason, both the sacred ministers and the other faithful must behave with great veneration and due diligence when celebrating them.

841
841. Since the sacraments are the same for the entire Church and belong to the divine deposit, it is exclusively up to the supreme authority of the Church to approve or define what is required for their validity, and to herself or another competent authority, in accordance with the c

842
842. § 1 . Whoever has not received baptism cannot be validly admitted to the other sacraments.
§ 2 . The sacraments of baptism, confirmation and the most holy Eucharist are so closely linked to each other that they are all necessary for full Christian initiation.

843
843. § 1 . Sacred ministers cannot deny the sacraments to those who request them in a timely manner, are well disposed, and are not prohibited by law from receiving them.
§ 2 The pastors of souls and the other faithful, each according to their ecclesiastical function, have the obligation to ensure that those who request the sacraments prepare to receive them with due evangelization and catechetical training, taking into account the norms given by the competent ecclesiastical authority.

844
844. § 1 . Catholic ministers licitly administer the sacraments only to the Catholic faithful, who, in turn, only receive them licitly from Catholic ministers, except as established in §§ 2, 3 and 4 of this canon, and in the c

845
845. § 1 . The sacraments of baptism, confirmation and orders imprint character and, therefore, cannot be reiterated.
§ 2 . If, after diligent investigation, prudent doubt remains as to whether the sacraments discussed in § 1 were actually received or were validly received, let them be administered under condition.

846
846. § 1 . In the celebration of the sacraments, the liturgical books approved by the competent authority must be faithfully observed; Therefore, no one adds, deletes or changes anything on their own initiative.
§ 2 . The minister must celebrate the sacraments according to his own rite.

847
847. § 1 . To administer the sacraments in which holy oils must be used, the minister must use olive oil or oil from other plants, recently consecrated or blessed by the Bishop, leaving aside what is prescribed in c. 999, 2; and the old ones should not be used if there is no need.
§ 2 . The parish priest must obtain the sacred oils from the Bishop himself and diligently store them in a decent place.

848
848. Apart from the oblations determined by the competent authority, the minister must not ask for anything for the administration of the sacraments, and must always ensure that those in need are not deprived of the help of the sacraments due to their poverty.

849
849. Baptism, the door of the sacraments, whose reception in fact or at least of desire is necessary for salvation, by which men are freed from sins, reborn as children of God and incorporated into the Church, being configured with Christ by the indelible character, is validly conferred only through ablution with true water accompanied by the due verbal form.

850
850. Baptism is administered according to the ritual prescribed in the approved liturgical books, except in cases of urgent need, in which only those things must be performed that are necessary for the validity of the sacrament.

851
851. The celebration of baptism must be adequately prepared; therefore:
1 the adult who wishes to receive baptism must be admitted to the catechumenate and, to the extent possible, be taken through successive steps to sacramental initiation, according to the initiation ritual adapted by the Episcopal Conference, and taking into account the peculiar norms dictated by it;
2 the parents of the child who is going to be baptized, and also those who will assume the role of godparents, must be suitably enlightened about the meaning of this sacrament and the obligations it entails; and the parish priest must ensure, personally or through other people, that the parents are duly instructed with pastoral exhortations and even with common prayer, bringing together several families, and visiting them where it is possible to do so.

852
852. § 1 . The provisions of the canons on adult baptism apply to all those who have passed from childhood and have the use of reason.
§ 2 . Also with regard to baptism, he who does not have the use of reason is assimilated to the infant.

853
853. Except in cases of necessity, the water used to administer baptism must be blessed according to the prescriptions of the liturgical books.

854
854. Baptism must be administered by immersion or infusion, in accordance with the norms of the Episcopal Conference.

855
855. Parents, godparents and the parish priest should ensure that a name foreign to Christian sentiment is not imposed.

856
856. Although baptism can be celebrated on any day, it is nevertheless advisable that, ordinarily, it be administered on Sunday or, if possible, on the Easter Vigil.

857
857. § 1 . Outside the case of necessity, the proper place for baptism is a church or oratory.
§ 2 . As a general rule, the adult should be baptized in his or her own parish church, and the child in the parish church of his or her parents, unless a just cause advises otherwise.

858
858. § 1 . Every parish church must have a baptismal font, preserving the cumulative right already acquired by other churches.
§ 2 . The Ordinary of the place, having heard the parish priest of the place in question, may allow or order that, for the convenience of the faithful, there is also a baptismal font in another church or oratory within the limits of the parish.
858. § 2, baptism can and should be conferred in another nearby church or oratory, or in another decent place.

859
859. If, due to remoteness or other circumstances, the person to be baptized cannot go or be taken without serious inconvenience to the parish church or to that other church or oratory in question in the c

860
860. § 1 . Outside of cases of necessity, baptism should not be administered in private homes, unless the local Ordinary has permitted it for serious reasons.
§ 2 . Unless the diocesan Bishop establishes otherwise, baptism should not be celebrated in hospitals, except in cases of necessity or when other pastoral reasons require it.

861
861. § 2 .
§ 2 . In case of necessity, or when a true spiritual utility advises it, and provided that the danger of error or indifferentism is avoided, it is permitted for the faithful for whom it is physically or morally impossible to go to a Catholic minister, to receive the sacraments of penance, Eucharist and anointing of the sick from those non-Catholic ministers, in whose Church those sacraments are valid.
§ 3 . Catholic ministers licitly administer the sacraments of penance, Eucharist and anointing of the sick to members of Eastern Churches that are not in full communion with the Catholic Church, if they request them spontaneously and are well disposed; and this norm also applies to the members of other Churches, which, in the opinion of the Apostolic See, are in the same condition as the aforementioned Eastern Churches, with regard to the sacraments.
§ 4 . If there is a danger of death or, in the opinion of the diocesan Bishop or the Episcopal Conference, another serious need is urgent, Catholic ministers can licitly administer those same sacraments also to other Christians who are not in full communion with the Catholic Church, when they cannot go to a minister of their own community and request it spontaneously, as long as they profess the Catholic faith regarding those sacraments and are well disposed.
§ 5 . For the cases excepted in §§ 2, 3 and 4, the diocesan Bishop or the Episcopal Conference must not give general norms without having consulted the authority, at least local, of the Church or non-Catholic community in question.
861. § 1 . Remaining in force what is prescribed in c. 530, 1, the Bishop, the priest and the deacon are ordinary ministers of baptism.
§ 2 . If the ordinary minister is absent or prevented, the baptism is licitly administered by a catechist or another designated for this function by the local Ordinary, and, in case of need, any person who has the proper intention; and pastors of souls, especially the parish priest, must ensure that the faithful know how to baptize properly.

862
862. Except in cases of necessity, it is lawful for no one to baptize in another's territory without due license, not even his subjects.

863
863. The baptism of adults, at least of those who have reached the age of fourteen, be offered to the Bishop so that he may administer it himself, if he considers it appropriate.

864
864. Any human being not yet baptized, and only him, is capable of receiving baptism.

865
865. § 1 . In order for an adult to be baptized, he or she must have expressed his or her desire to receive this sacrament, be sufficiently instructed in the truths of faith and Christian obligations, and have been tested in the Christian life through the catechumenate; He must also be exhorted to grieve for his sins.
§ 2 . An adult who is in danger of death may be baptized if, having some knowledge of the main truths of the faith, he expresses in any way his intention to receive baptism and promises that he will observe the commandments of the Christian religion.

866
866. Unless a serious cause obstructs, the adult who is baptized must be confirmed immediately after baptism and participate in the Eucharistic celebration, also receiving communion.

867
867. § 1 . Parents have the obligation to have their children baptized in the first weeks; As soon as possible after birth and even before it, go to the priest to ask for the sacrament for your child and prepare properly.
§ 2 . If the child is in danger of death, he must be baptized without delay.

868
868. § 1 . To licitly baptize a child, it is required:
1 that the parents, or at least one of the two, or those who legitimately act in their place, give their consent;
2 n that there is a well-founded hope that the child will be educated in the Catholic religion, without prejudice to the § 3; If this hope is completely lacking, the baptism must be deferred, according to the provisions of particular law, making the reason known to the parents.
§ 2 . The child of Catholic parents, and even of non-Catholic parents, in danger of death, can lawfully be baptized, even against the will of his parents.
§ 3 . n The child of non-Catholic Christians may be licitly baptized if the parents or at least one of them or the person who legitimately occupies their place request it and if it is impossible for them, physically or morally, to access their own minister.

869
869. § 1 . When there is doubt as to whether someone was baptized, or whether baptism was validly administered, and the doubt remains after careful investigation, he is to be baptized under condition.
§ 2 . Those baptized in a non-Catholic ecclesial community should not be baptized under condition, unless there is serious reason to doubt the validity of their baptism, taking into account both the material and the formula used in its administration, and the intention of the baptized person, if they were an adult, and of the minister.
§ 3 . If, in the cases dealt with in §§ 1 and 2, there is doubt about the administration of baptism or its validity, the sacrament should not be administered before the doctrine about it has been taught to the person who is to receive it, if he is an adult, and the reasons why the validity of the baptism previously celebrated has been expressed to him, or to his parents if it is an infant.

870
870. A foundling or abandoned child must be baptized, unless its baptism is established after a diligent investigation.

871
871. To the extent possible, aborted fetuses should be baptized, if they are alive.
(n Indicates that the text corresponds to the new version)
[Original wording of the canons modified by His Holiness Pope Francis (cf. Apostolic Letter in the form of Motu Proprio De concordia inter Codices, dated May 31, 2016)]:
2 that there is a well-founded hope that the child will be educated in the Catholic religion; If this hope is completely lacking, the baptism must be deferred, according to the provisions of particular law, making the parents aware of the reason.

872
872. To the extent possible, the person who is going to receive baptism must be given a godfather, whose role is to assist the adult who is baptized in his Christian initiation, and, together with the parents, present the child who is going to receive baptism and ensure that he then leads a Christian life consistent with baptism and faithfully fulfills the obligations inherent to it.

873
873. Have only one godfather or one godmother, or one and both.

874
874. § 1 . For someone to be admitted as a sponsor, it is necessary that:
1 has been chosen by the person who is going to be baptized or by his parents or by those who occupy his place or, in the absence of these, by the parish priest or minister; and that it has the capacity for this mission and the intention to carry it out;
2 has reached the age of sixteen, unless the diocesan Bishop establishes another age, or unless, for just cause, the parish priest or minister considers an exception admissible;
3 is Catholic, is confirmed, has already received the most holy sacrament of the Eucharist and leads, at the same time, a life consistent with the faith and the mission that he or she is going to assume;
4 is not affected by a canonical penalty, legitimately imposed or declared;
5 is not the father or mother of the one to be baptized.
§ 2 . The baptized person who belongs to a non-Catholic ecclesial community can only be admitted together with a Catholic godfather, and exclusively as a witness to the baptism.

875
875. Whoever administers the baptism ensures that, if the godfather is missing, there is at least one witness by whom his administration can be proven.

876
876. If no harm is caused to anyone, to prove baptism the statement of a single witness immune from all suspicion is sufficient, or the oath of the baptized person himself, if he received the sacrament as an adult.

877
877. § 1 . The parish priest of the place where the baptism is celebrated must diligently and without delay write down in the baptism book the name of the baptized, mentioning the minister, the parents, godparents, witnesses, if any, and the place and day on which it was administered, also indicating the day and place of the birth.
§ 2 . When it is a child of a single mother, the mother's name must be registered, if her maternity is publicly stated or she herself requests it voluntarily in writing or before two witnesses; and the name of the father must also be registered, if his paternity is proven by a public document or by his own statement before the parish priest and two witnesses; In other cases, only the name of the baptized person will be registered, without mentioning the name of the father or parents at all.
§ 3 . If it is an adopted child, the name of those who adopted it will be registered and also, at least if this is done in the civil registry of the region, that of the natural parents, as established in §§ 1 and 2, taking into account the provisions of the Episcopal Conference.

878
878. If the baptism was not administered by the parish priest nor while he was present, the minister, whoever he may be, must inform the parish priest of that parish in which the sacrament was administered, so that he can make the registration as indicated in the c

879
879. The sacrament of confirmation, which imprints character and by which the baptized, advancing along the path of Christian initiation, are enriched with the gift of the Holy Spirit and linked more perfectly to the Church, strengthens and forces them with greater force to, in word and deed, be witnesses of Christ and propagate and defend the faith.

880
880. § 1 . The sacrament of confirmation is administered by anointing with chrism on the forehead, which is done with the laying on of the hand, and by the words prescribed in the approved liturgical books.
§ 2 . The chrism that must be used in confirmation must be consecrated by the Bishop, even if it is a priest who administers the sacrament.

881
881. It is appropriate that the sacrament of confirmation be celebrated in a church and within the Mass; However, for just and reasonable cause, it can be celebrated outside of Mass and in any decent place.

882
882. The ordinary minister of confirmation is the Bishop; This sacrament is also validly administered by the priest endowed with faculty by universal law or by peculiar concession of the competent authority.

883
883. They enjoy ipso iure the power to confirm:
1 within the limits of their jurisdiction, who in law are equal to the diocesan Bishop;
2 with respect to the person in question, the priest who, by reason of his office or by order of the diocesan Bishop, baptizes one who has outlived childhood, or admits one already baptized into the full communion of the Catholic Church;
3 for those who are in danger of death, the parish priest, and even any priest.

884
884. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop must administer confirmation himself, or see to it that another Bishop administers it; But if necessity requires it, he can grant power to one or more specific priests to administer this sacrament.
§ 2 . For serious reasons, the Bishop, and also the priest endowed with the power to confirm by law or by concession of the competent authority, may, in particular cases, associate other priests who also administer the sacrament.

885
885. § 1 . The diocesan Bishop has the obligation to ensure that the sacrament of confirmation is administered to his subjects who duly and reasonably request it.
§ 2 . The priest who enjoys this faculty must use it towards those in whose favor the faculty has been granted.

886
886. § 1 . Within his diocese, the Bishop legitimately administers the sacrament of confirmation also to those faithful who are not his subjects, unless expressly prohibited by his own Ordinary.
§ 2 . To licitly administer confirmation in a foreign diocese, a Bishop needs permission from the diocesan Bishop, at least reasonably presumed, unless it concerns his own subjects.

887
887. Within the territory designated to him, the priest who enjoys the faculty of confirmation can licitly administer this sacrament also to strangers, unless prohibited by his own Ordinary; but, keeping in mind what is prescribed in c. 883, 3, cannot validly administer it to anyone in another's territory.

888
888. Within the territory in which they are empowered to confirm, ministers can administer this sacrament also in exempt places.

889
889. § 1 . Only every baptized person not yet confirmed is capable of receiving confirmation.
§ 2 . Outside of the danger of death, for someone to lawfully receive confirmation it is required that, if they have the use of reason, they are suitably instructed, well disposed and able to renew the promises of baptism.

890
890. The faithful are obliged to receive this sacrament at the appropriate time; Parents and pastors of souls, especially parish priests, ensure that the faithful are well prepared to receive it and that they receive it in a timely manner.

891
891. The sacrament of confirmation must be administered to the faithful around the age of discretion, unless the Episcopal Conference determines another age, or there is a danger of death or, in the opinion of the minister, a serious cause advises otherwise.

892
892. To the extent possible, the confirming party should have a godfather, whose responsibility it is to ensure that he behaves as a true witness of Christ and faithfully fulfills the obligations inherent to the sacrament.

893
893. § 1 . In order for someone to be a godfather, it is necessary that they meet the conditions expressed in c. 874.
§ 2 . It is convenient that the person who assumed that mission at baptism be chosen as godfather.

894
894. To prove the administration of confirmation, observe the prescriptions of c. 876.

895
895. The names of those confirmed must be registered in the confirmation book of the diocesan Curia, leaving a record of the minister, the parents and godparents, and the place and day of the administration of the sacrament, or, where ordered by the Episcopal Conference or the diocesan Bishop, in the book that must be kept in the parish archive; The parish priest must notify the parish priest of the place of baptism, so that the entry can be made in the baptism book in accordance with the c

896
896. If the local priest has not been present, the minister must, by himself or through another, communicate to him as soon as possible the confirmation administered.

897
897. The most august sacrament, in which Christ Our Lord himself is contained, offered and received, is the most holy Eucharist, by which the Church lives and continually grows. The Eucharistic Sacrifice, memorial of the death and resurrection of the Lord, in which the Sacrifice of the Cross is perpetuated throughout the centuries, is the culmination and source of all worship and of all Christian life, by which the unity of the people of God is signified and realized and the building of the body of Christ is brought to completion. Thus the other sacraments and all the ecclesiastical works of apostolate are closely united to the most holy Eucharist and are ordered to it.

898
898. Let the faithful pay the greatest veneration to the most holy Eucharist, taking an active part in the celebration of the most august Sacrifice, receiving this sacrament frequently and with great devotion, and worshiping it with utmost adoration; Pastors of souls, when expounding the doctrine on this sacrament, diligently instill this obligation in the faithful.

899
899. § 1 . The Eucharistic celebration is an action of Christ himself and of the Church, in which Christ Our Lord, substantially present under the species of bread and wine, through the ministry of the priest, offers himself to God the Father, and is given as spiritual food to the faithful united to his oblation.
§ 2 . In the Eucharistic Assembly, presided over by the Bishop, or by a priest under his authority, who act by personifying Christ, the people of God gather in unity, and all the faithful who attend, both clerics and laymen, take an active part, each one according to their own way, in accordance with the diversity of orders and liturgical functions.
§ 3 . The Eucharistic celebration must be arranged in such a way that all those who participate in it receive abundant fruits, for which Christ Our Lord instituted the Eucharistic Sacrifice.

900
900. § 1 . Only the validly ordained priest is a minister capable of preparing the sacrament of the Eucharist, acting in the person of Christ.
